self-importance
/ˌself ɪmˈpɔːtns/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：妄自尊大、自以为了不起。
① His self-importance annoyed everyone in the office, especially when he called himself "the boss".
= 他妄自尊大，惹恼了办公室里的每个人，尤其是他自称「老板」的时候。
self-importance = 妄自尊大
② The comedian makes fun of the self-importance of politicians who think they know everything.
= 那位喜剧演员嘲讽那些自以为无所不知、妄自尊大的政客。
the self-importance of somebody = 某人的妄自尊大

self-important
/ˌself ɪmˈpɔːtnt/
核心：形容词（贬义）：妄自尊大的、自以为了不起的。副词 self-importantly 是自以为了不起地。
① A self-important little man at the desk told us we would have to wait for an hour.
= 前台一个自以为了不起的小个子男人告诉我们得等一个小时。
self-important = 妄自尊大的
② The young manager walked self-importantly around the office, giving orders to everyone.
= 那个年轻经理自以为了不起地在办公室里走来走去，对每个人发号施令。
self-importantly = 自以为了不起地

self-imposed
/ˌself ɪmˈpəʊzd/
核心：形容词：自己强加的、自愿承担的：a self-imposed deadline、self-imposed exile。
① The writer set himself a self-imposed deadline of one chapter a week.
= 那位作家给自己定了个期限：每周写一章。
a self-imposed deadline = 自定的期限
self-imposed = 自己强加的
② After the scandal, the singer went into self-imposed exile in a small village in France.
= 丑闻过后，那位歌手自我放逐到法国的一个小村子里。
self-imposed exile = 自我放逐

self-improvement
/ˌself ɪmˈpruːvmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我完善、自我提高。
① He spends his evenings on self-improvement, learning Spanish and reading history books.
= 他晚上都用来自我提高，学西班牙语、读历史书。
self-improvement = 自我提高
② The bookshop has a large section of books on self-improvement and healthy living.
= 那家书店有一大块区域专卖自我提高和健康生活方面的书。
books on self-improvement = 自我提高方面的书

self-induced
/ˌself ɪnˈdjuːst/
核心：形容词：自己造成的、自找的：self-induced problems。
① Many of his health problems were self-induced, caused by years of smoking and drinking.
= 他的许多健康问题是自己造成的，是多年抽烟喝酒的结果。
self-induced = 自己造成的
② The team's defeat was self-induced, because they gave away two easy goals.
= 那支球队的失败是自找的，因为他们送了对方两个轻松的进球。
a self-induced defeat = 自找的失败

self-indulgence
/ˌself ɪnˈdʌldʒəns/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自我放纵、放任自己。
① Buying a new car every year is pure self-indulgence when you only drive to the shops.
= 只是开车去商店，却每年都买新车，纯属放纵自己。
self-indulgence = 自我放纵
② A box of chocolates on a Friday evening is my one small self-indulgence.
= 星期五晚上吃一盒巧克力，是我唯一的一点小放纵。
a small self-indulgence = 小小的放纵

self-indulgent
/ˌself ɪnˈdʌldʒənt/
核心：形容词（贬义）：放纵自己的、只顾自己享受的；（作品）任性的、自我陶醉的。
① It felt a bit self-indulgent to stay in bed until noon, but I was very tired.
= 一直赖床到中午感觉有点放纵自己，可我真的很累。
self-indulgent = 放纵自己的
② Critics called the film long and self-indulgent, full of scenes that the director loved but nobody else did.
= 评论家说那部电影冗长而自我陶醉，满是导演自己喜欢、别人都不喜欢的镜头。
a self-indulgent film = 自我陶醉的电影

self-inflicted
/ˌself ɪnˈflɪktɪd/
核心：形容词：（伤害、问题）自己造成的、自讨的：a self-inflicted wound。
① The doctors said the wound was self-inflicted, and the soldier admitted that he had shot himself.
= 医生说那伤是他自己造成的，那名士兵也承认是自己开的枪。
a self-inflicted wound = 自伤
self-inflicted = 自己造成的
② The company's problems are largely self-inflicted, caused by bad decisions at the top.
= 那家公司的问题主要是自找的，是高层决策失误造成的。
largely self-inflicted = 主要是自找的

self-interest
/ˌself ˈɪntrəst/
核心：名词，不可数：私利、自身利益。
① He only helped us out of self-interest, because he wanted us to vote for him.
= 他帮我们只是出于私利，因为他想让我们投他的票。
out of self-interest = 出于私利
self-interest = 私利
② It is in our own self-interest to protect the river, because the town depends on it.
= 保护那条河符合我们自身的利益，因为全镇都靠它。
in your own self-interest = 符合自身利益

self-interested
/ˌself ˈɪntrəstɪd/
核心：形容词（常含贬义）：自私自利的、只顾私利的。
① The decision was self-interested, made to protect the managers rather than the workers.
= 那个决定是自私自利的，是为了保护经理而不是工人。
self-interested = 自私自利的
② Voters are tired of self-interested politicians who forget their promises after the election.
= 选民厌倦了那些只顾私利、选后就忘了承诺的政客。
self-interested politicians = 只顾私利的政客

self-involved
/ˌself ɪnˈvɒlvd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只关心自己的、自我陶醉的。
① He was too self-involved to notice that his friend was having a hard time.
= 他太只顾自己了，没注意到朋友正过得很艰难。
self-involved = 只关心自己的
② The novel's main character is a self-involved young man who learns to care about others.
= 那部小说的主人公是一个只顾自己的年轻人，后来学会了关心别人。
a self-involved young man = 只顾自己的年轻人

self-knowledge
/ˌself ˈnɒlɪdʒ/
核心：名词，不可数：自知之明、对自己的了解。
① Self-knowledge comes with age, as we learn what makes us happy and what makes us angry.
= 自知之明随年龄而来，我们会慢慢明白什么让自己快乐、什么让自己生气。
self-knowledge = 自知之明
② The course helps managers gain self-knowledge about how they deal with stress.
= 那门课程帮助管理者更了解自己如何应对压力。
gain self-knowledge = 加深对自己的了解

self-loathing
/ˌself ˈləʊðɪŋ/
核心：名词，不可数：自我厌恶、自我憎恨。
① After he hurt his friend, he was filled with self-loathing for weeks.
= 伤害了朋友之后，他好几个星期都充满了自我厌恶。
be filled with self-loathing = 充满自我厌恶
self-loathing = 自我厌恶
② The singer wrote about the self-loathing she felt as a teenager in her new song.
= 那位歌手在新歌里写了她少女时代感受到的自我厌恶。
feel self-loathing = 感到自我厌恶

self-love
/ˌself ˈlʌv/
核心：名词，不可数：自爱；（贬义）自恋、自私。
① The book teaches that a little self-love is necessary before you can love other people.
= 那本书讲，你要先有一点自爱，才能去爱别人。
self-love = 自爱
② His self-love was so great that he could not accept any criticism at all.
= 他自恋到了极点，一点批评都接受不了。
self-love = 自恋

self-made
/ˌself ˈmeɪd/
核心：形容词：白手起家的、靠自己奋斗成功的：a self-made man / millionaire。
① My grandfather was a self-made man who started with nothing and built a big company.
= 我祖父是个白手起家的人，从一无所有做到创办了一家大公司。
a self-made man = 白手起家的人
self-made = 白手起家的
② She is a self-made millionaire who left school at sixteen and opened her first shop at twenty.
= 她是白手起家的百万富翁，十六岁辍学，二十岁开了第一家店。
a self-made millionaire = 白手起家的百万富翁

self-management
/ˌself ˈmænɪdʒmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我管理；（疾病的）自我照护。
① Self-management is an important skill for people who work from home without a boss.
= 对没有老板、在家工作的人来说，自我管理是一项重要技能。
self-management = 自我管理
② At the clinic, the nurse teaches patients with diabetes the self-management of their condition.
= 在诊所里，那位护士教糖尿病人自我照护自己的病情。
the self-management of a condition = 病情的自我照护

self-medicate
/ˌself ˈmedɪkeɪt/
核心：动词，不及物：自行用药：不经医生自己吃药；（贬义）借酒、药物逃避问题。
① Many people self-medicate with painkillers instead of going to see a doctor.
= 许多人不去看医生，而是自己吃止痛药。
self-medicate = 自行用药
② After his divorce, he started to self-medicate with alcohol to forget his problems.
= 离婚后，他开始借酒浇愁，忘掉烦恼。
self-medicate with alcohol = 借酒逃避

self-motivated
/ˌself ˈməʊtɪveɪtɪd/
核心：形容词：自我激励的、积极主动的：不需要别人督促。
① We are looking for a self-motivated person who can work alone and solve problems.
= 我们在找一个积极主动、能独立工作和解决问题的人。
a self-motivated person = 积极主动的人
self-motivated = 自我激励的
② Students who study online need to be self-motivated, because nobody checks their work every day.
= 上网课的学生需要积极主动，因为没人每天检查他们的作业。
be self-motivated = 积极主动

self-motivation
/ˌself ˌməʊtɪˈveɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我激励、自觉性。
① Running a marathon takes months of training and a lot of self-motivation.
= 跑马拉松需要几个月的训练和很强的自我激励。
self-motivation = 自我激励
② Her self-motivation impressed her teachers, who never had to tell her to do her homework.
= 她的自觉性给老师们留下了深刻印象，他们从来不用叫她做作业。
somebody's self-motivation = 某人的自觉性

self-mutilation
/ˌself ˌmjuːtɪˈleɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自残、自伤肢体。
① The young prisoner was moved to a hospital after several acts of self-mutilation in his cell.
= 那名年轻囚犯在牢房里几次自残后，被转到了医院。
acts of self-mutilation = 自残行为
self-mutilation = 自残
② Doctors say self-mutilation is often a sign of deep pain that a person cannot talk about.
= 医生说，自残往往是一个人说不出口的深层痛苦的信号。
self-mutilation is a sign = 自残是一种信号

self-obsessed
/ˌself əbˈsest/
核心：形容词（贬义）：极度自我关注的、只关心自己的。
① The film is about a self-obsessed actor who cannot stop looking at himself in the mirror.
= 那部电影讲一个极度自恋、一直照镜子停不下来的演员。
self-obsessed = 极度自我关注的
② Some people say that social media is making young people more self-obsessed.
= 有人说社交媒体正让年轻人越来越只关心自己。
more self-obsessed = 越来越只关心自己

self-opinionated
/ˌself əˈpɪnjəneɪtɪd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自以为是的、固执己见的。
① He is a loud, self-opinionated man who never listens to anyone else's ideas.
= 他嗓门大、自以为是，从来不听别人的意见。
self-opinionated = 自以为是的
② The self-opinionated critic wrote that nobody else understood the film except him.
= 那个自以为是的评论家写道，除了他谁都看不懂那部电影。
a self-opinionated critic = 自以为是的评论家

self-perpetuating
/ˌself pəˈpetʃueɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我延续的、恶性循环的：一旦开始就会自己持续下去。
① Poverty can be self-perpetuating, because poor children often have fewer chances at school.
= 贫穷可能会自我延续，因为穷孩子在学校的机会往往更少。
self-perpetuating = 自我延续的
② Fear of crime is self-perpetuating: people stay at home, so the streets become emptier and less safe.
= 对犯罪的恐惧会形成恶性循环：人们待在家里，街上就更空、更不安全。
be self-perpetuating = 形成恶性循环

self-pity
/ˌself ˈpɪti/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自怜、自哀自怨。
① Instead of drowning in self-pity after losing his job, he started his own small business.
= 他失业后没有沉溺于自怜，而是自己开了一家小公司。
drown in self-pity = 沉溺于自怜
self-pity = 自怜
常带批评口气，说人沉溺于自己的不幸、不肯振作。
② There was a note of self-pity in her voice when she talked about her unhappy childhood.
= 她谈到不幸的童年时，声音里带着几分自怜。
a note of self-pity = 几分自怜

self-pitying
/ˌself ˈpɪtiɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自怜的、自哀自怨的。
① He wrote a long self-pitying letter about how badly everyone had treated him.
= 他写了一封长长的自怜信，说大家对他有多坏。
a self-pitying letter = 自怜的信
self-pitying = 自怜的
② Stop being so self-pitying all the time and do something to change the situation.
= 别总那么自哀自怨了，做点什么改变现状吧。
be self-pitying = 自哀自怨

self-portrait
/ˌself ˈpɔːtreɪt/
核心：名词，可数：自画像；（比喻）自我描写。
① The museum has a self-portrait that the artist painted when he was twenty and very poor.
= 那座博物馆有一幅那位画家二十岁、穷困潦倒时画的自画像。
a self-portrait = 自画像
② Her first novel is really a self-portrait of a lonely girl growing up in the 1970s.
= 她的第一部小说其实是一幅自我写照，写的是二十世纪七十年代一个孤独女孩的成长。
a self-portrait of somebody = 某人的自我写照

self-possessed
/ˌself pəˈzest/
核心：形容词：沉着冷静的、镇定自若的。
① For a girl of fifteen, she was very self-possessed and answered the reporters' questions calmly.
= 作为一个十五岁的女孩，她非常镇定，从容地回答了记者的提问。
self-possessed = 沉着冷静的
② He remained self-possessed even when the angry crowd started shouting at him.
= 即使愤怒的人群开始冲他大喊，他依然镇定自若。
remain self-possessed = 保持镇定

self-possession
/ˌself pəˈzeʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：沉着、镇定。
① She showed great self-possession when the fire alarm went off in the middle of her speech.
= 她演讲到一半火警响了，她表现得非常镇定。
show self-possession = 表现得镇定
self-possession = 沉着
② He lost his self-possession for a moment, then smiled and continued as if nothing had happened.
= 他一时失了镇定，随即笑了笑，像什么都没发生一样继续下去。
lose your self-possession = 失去镇定

self-preservation
/ˌself ˌprezəˈveɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我保护、自保：the instinct of self-preservation。
① A strong instinct of self-preservation made him jump out of the way of the car.
= 强烈的自保本能让他一下子跳开，躲过了那辆车。
the instinct of self-preservation = 自保本能
self-preservation = 自我保护
② The minister lied to parliament out of self-preservation, hoping to keep his job.
= 那位部长出于自保对议会撒了谎，希望保住职位。
out of self-preservation = 出于自保

self-proclaimed
/ˌself prəˈkleɪmd/
核心：形容词，只作定语（常含贬义）：自封的、自称的：a self-proclaimed leader。
① The self-proclaimed king of the island had no real power at all.
= 那个自封为岛上国王的人根本没有任何实权。
self-proclaimed = 自封的
② My brother, a self-proclaimed expert on football, tells everyone who will win every match.
= 我哥哥自封为足球专家，逢人就说每场比赛谁会赢。
a self-proclaimed expert = 自封的专家

self-promotion
/ˌself prəˈməʊʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自我宣传、自我推销。
① The singer's constant self-promotion on social media annoyed some of her fans.
= 那位歌手在社交媒体上没完没了的自我宣传惹恼了一些歌迷。
self-promotion = 自我宣传
② In some jobs, a little self-promotion is necessary if you want to be noticed.
= 在有些工作里，想被人注意到，就得有点自我推销。
a little self-promotion = 一点自我推销

self-realization
/ˌself ˌriːəlaɪˈzeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我实现、实现自我潜能。英式也拼作 self-realisation。
① For the philosopher, education was a path to self-realization, not just a way to get a job.
= 对那位哲学家来说，教育是通往自我实现的道路，而不只是找工作的手段。
self-realization = 自我实现
② Many people find self-realization in art, music, gardening or caring for others.
= 许多人在艺术、音乐、园艺或照顾他人中实现自我。
find self-realization = 实现自我

self-referential
/ˌself ˌrefəˈrenʃl/
核心：形容词：自我指涉的、指向自身的：（作品）谈论或模仿自身的。
① The television comedy is very self-referential, with jokes about its own actors and earlier episodes.
= 那部电视喜剧很爱自我指涉，常拿自己的演员和前几集开玩笑。
self-referential = 自我指涉的
② Some modern novels are self-referential, with characters who know they are in a book.
= 有些现代小说会指涉自身，里面的人物知道自己在一本书里。
be self-referential = 指涉自身

self-reflection
/ˌself rɪˈflekʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我反省、反躬自省。
① The long walk in the mountains gave her time for self-reflection after a difficult year.
= 经历了艰难的一年，在山里长途徒步让她有时间反省自己。
time for self-reflection = 自我反省的时间
self-reflection = 自我反省
② Writing a diary every night encourages self-reflection and helps you understand your feelings.
= 每晚写日记能促进自我反省，帮你理解自己的情绪。
encourage self-reflection = 促进自我反省

self-regard
/ˌself rɪˈɡɑːd/
核心：名词，不可数：自尊；（贬义）自负、过分看重自己。
① Doing well at school helped to build the boy's self-regard after years of failure.
= 多年失败之后，在学校表现好帮那个男孩建立了自尊。
self-regard = 自尊
② The actor's enormous self-regard made him very difficult to work with on the film.
= 那个演员极为自负，拍片时很难与之共事。
enormous self-regard = 极度自负

self-regarding
/ˌself rɪˈɡɑːdɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只顾自己的、自私的。
① The critic called the book self-regarding, because the writer only talked about his own feelings.
= 那位评论家说那本书只顾自我，因为作者只谈自己的感受。
self-regarding = 只顾自己的
② She was tired of his self-regarding stories about how clever he was at work.
= 她厌倦了他那些讲自己工作上有多聪明的自吹故事。
self-regarding stories = 自吹自擂的故事

self-regulating
/ˌself ˈreɡjuleɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我调节的、自律的：a self-regulating industry。
① The body is a self-regulating system that keeps its temperature at about thirty-seven degrees.
= 人体是一个自我调节的系统，能把体温保持在三十七度左右。
a self-regulating system = 自我调节的系统
self-regulating = 自我调节的
② The newspapers want to remain self-regulating rather than be controlled by the government.
= 报业希望保持自律，而不是受政府管控。
remain self-regulating = 保持自律

self-regulation
/ˌself ˌreɡjuˈleɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：（行业的）自律、自我监管；（个人的）自我调节。
① Critics say that self-regulation of the banks failed to stop the financial crisis.
= 批评者说，银行业的自律没能阻止那场金融危机。
self-regulation = 自律
② Young children are still learning self-regulation and often cannot control their anger.
= 幼儿还在学习自我调节，常常控制不住怒气。
learn self-regulation = 学习自我调节

self-reliance
/ˌself rɪˈlaɪəns/
核心：名词，不可数：自立、自力更生。
① Living alone in the countryside for a year taught her self-reliance and practical skills.
= 独自在乡下住了一年，教会了她自立和实用的本领。
self-reliance = 自立
多用于个人，也可说国家经济的自力更生。
② The school encourages self-reliance by letting older children organize their own trips.
= 那所学校让高年级学生自己组织出游，鼓励他们自立。
encourage self-reliance = 鼓励自立

self-report
/ˌself rɪˈpɔːt/
核心：名词，可数：（研究中的）自我报告、自述。动词：自我报告。多作定语：self-report data。
① The study is based on self-report, so people may not always tell the truth about how much they drink.
= 那项研究基于自我报告，所以人们未必总会如实说自己喝多少酒。
self-report = 自我报告
② Patients self-report their pain on a scale from one to ten every morning.
= 病人每天早上按一到十的等级自己报告疼痛程度。
self-report = 自我报告（动词）

self-respect
/ˌself rɪˈspekt/
核心：名词，不可数：自尊、自重。
① Losing his job took away his self-respect, and he stopped looking after himself.
= 失业夺走了他的自尊，他也不再照顾自己了。
self-respect = 自尊
偏「自重、有骨气」，指不肯做降低身份、有失尊严的事。
② No one with any self-respect would accept such a rude offer from the company.
= 有点自尊心的人都不会接受那家公司那么无礼的条件。
anyone with self-respect = 有自尊心的人

self-respecting
/ˌself rɪˈspektɪŋ/
核心：形容词，只作定语：自重的、有自尊的；（幽默）够格的、像样的：no self-respecting cat would…。
① No self-respecting chef would serve frozen vegetables in an expensive restaurant like this.
= 稍有自尊的厨师都不会在这样的高档餐馆里上冷冻蔬菜。
no self-respecting chef = 稍有自尊的厨师
self-respecting = 自重的
② Every self-respecting teenager wants the latest phone, according to the advertisements on television.
= 按电视广告的说法，每个像样的青少年都想要最新款的手机。
every self-respecting teenager = 每个像样的青少年

self-restraint
/ˌself rɪˈstreɪnt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我克制、自制。
① It took a lot of self-restraint not to eat the whole box of chocolates at once.
= 忍住不一口气吃光一整盒巧克力，需要很强的自制力。
self-restraint = 自我克制
② The police showed great self-restraint when the protesters threw bottles at them.
= 抗议者朝警察扔瓶子时，警察表现得非常克制。
show self-restraint = 表现得克制

self-righteous
/ˌself ˈraɪtʃəs/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自以为是的、自命清高的：觉得只有自己对、道德比别人高。副词 self-righteously 是自以为是地。名词 self-righteousness 另立条。
① I hate his self-righteous tone when he tells everyone else how they should live.
= 他告诉别人该怎么生活时那种自以为是的口气，我很讨厌。
a self-righteous tone = 自以为是的口气
self-righteous = 自以为是的
② "I would never do such a thing," she said self-righteously, although we all knew she had.
= 「我绝不会做那种事。」她自命清高地说，可我们都知道她做过。
self-righteously = 自以为是地

self-righteousness
/ˌself ˈraɪtʃəsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：自以为是、自命清高。
① The self-righteousness of the new manager made him unpopular with the older staff.
= 新经理的自以为是让老员工很不待见他。
self-righteousness = 自以为是
② The film makes fun of the self-righteousness of people who judge others but ignore their own faults.
= 那部电影嘲讽了那些评判别人、却无视自己毛病的人的自命清高。
the self-righteousness of somebody = 某人的自命清高

self-rule
/ˌself ˈruːl/
核心：名词，不可数：自治：一个国家或地区自己管理自己。
① After a hundred years of foreign control, the country finally won self-rule in 1960.
= 经过一百年的外国统治，那个国家终于在一九六〇年赢得了自治。
win self-rule = 赢得自治
self-rule = 自治
② The islanders voted for self-rule, but they wanted to keep close ties with the mainland.
= 岛民们投票赞成自治，但希望和大陆保持密切联系。
vote for self-rule = 投票赞成自治

self-sacrifice
/ˌself ˈsækrɪfaɪs/
核心：名词，不可数：自我牺牲：为了别人放弃自己的利益。
① Her parents' self-sacrifice made it possible for her to study medicine at a good university.
= 她父母的自我牺牲让她得以在一所好大学学医。
self-sacrifice = 自我牺牲
② The film celebrates the self-sacrifice of the nurses who worked through the war.
= 那部电影颂扬了在战争中坚持工作的护士们的自我牺牲。
the self-sacrifice of somebody = 某人的自我牺牲

self-sacrificing
/ˌself ˈsækrɪfaɪsɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我牺牲的、舍己为人的。
① She was a kind, self-sacrificing woman who always put her family before herself.
= 她是个善良、舍己为人的女人，总是把家人放在自己前面。
self-sacrificing = 舍己为人的
② The self-sacrificing doctor gave up her holidays to work in the poorest parts of the city.
= 那位舍己为人的医生放弃了假期，到城里最穷的地方工作。
a self-sacrificing doctor = 舍己为人的医生

self-satisfaction
/ˌself ˌsætɪsˈfækʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自满、沾沾自喜。
① He smiled with obvious self-satisfaction when the boss praised his report in front of everyone.
= 老板当众表扬他的报告时，他明显沾沾自喜地笑了。
with self-satisfaction = 沾沾自喜地
self-satisfaction = 自满
② There was no room for self-satisfaction, because the team had only won by a single point.
= 没有自满的余地，因为球队只赢了一分。
no room for self-satisfaction = 没有自满的余地

self-satisfied
/ˌself ˈsætɪsfaɪd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自满的、沾沾自喜的：a self-satisfied smile。
① He had a self-satisfied smile on his face after he won the argument.
= 他吵赢之后，脸上带着沾沾自喜的笑容。
a self-satisfied smile = 沾沾自喜的笑容
self-satisfied = 自满的
② The government report strongly criticized the self-satisfied attitude of the company's managers.
= 那份政府报告严厉批评了公司管理层自满的态度。
a self-satisfied attitude = 自满的态度

self-seeking
/ˌself ˈsiːkɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：追逐私利的、自私的。名词，不可数：追逐私利。
① The voters were tired of self-seeking politicians who cared only about their own careers.
= 选民厌倦了那些只关心自己仕途、追逐私利的政客。
self-seeking politicians = 追逐私利的政客
self-seeking = 追逐私利的
② The charity was damaged by the self-seeking of a few of its leaders.
= 那家慈善机构因少数领导人追逐私利而受损。
the self-seeking of somebody = 某人的追逐私利

self-selected
/ˌself sɪˈlektɪd/
核心：形容词：（样本、人群）自选的、自愿加入的（而非随机抽取）。
① The survey used a self-selected group of people who chose to answer the questions online.
= 那项调查用的是一组自愿在网上答题的人。
a self-selected group = 自选的群体
self-selected = 自选的
② Results from a self-selected sample may not show what the whole population thinks.
= 自选样本得出的结果未必代表全体人口的想法。
a self-selected sample = 自选样本

self-selecting
/ˌself sɪˈlektɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自行选择的、自愿参与的：self-selecting groups。
① The class for advanced students is self-selecting, because only the keenest choose to join it.
= 那个高级班是自愿参加的，因为只有最积极的学生才会选。
self-selecting = 自愿参与的
② Online polls are self-selecting, so they often attract people with very strong opinions.
= 网上投票是自愿参加的，所以常常吸引观点非常强烈的人。
self-selecting polls = 自愿参与的投票

self-selection
/ˌself sɪˈlekʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我选择：（研究中）参与者自行决定是否参加，可能造成偏差。
① Self-selection is a problem in the study, because only healthy people chose to take part.
= 自我选择是那项研究的一个问题，因为只有健康的人选择参加。
self-selection = 自我选择
② The researchers tried to avoid self-selection by choosing people at random from the phone book.
= 研究人员从电话簿里随机挑选参与者，设法避免自我选择偏差。
avoid self-selection = 避免自我选择偏差

self-service
/ˌself ˈsɜːvɪs/
核心：形容词：自助式的：a self-service restaurant、self-service checkouts。名词，不可数：自助（服务）。
① We had lunch in a self-service restaurant where you carry your own tray to the table.
= 我们在一家自助餐厅吃了午饭，那里要自己端着托盘到桌边。
a self-service restaurant = 自助餐厅
self-service = 自助式的
② Most petrol stations now are self-service, so you fill the car yourself and pay inside.
= 现在大多数加油站都是自助的，你自己加油，然后进去付钱。
be self-service = 是自助的

self-serving
/ˌself ˈsɜːvɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只为自己谋利的、自私自利的。
① The minister's self-serving explanation for the mistake did not convince anyone in parliament.
= 那位部长对失误的辩解只为自己开脱，没能说服议会里的任何人。
a self-serving explanation = 只为自己开脱的辩解
self-serving = 自私自利的
② Many voters see the new law as self-serving, because it protects the politicians who wrote it.
= 许多选民认为那项新法律是为自己谋利，因为它保护的正是起草它的政客。
be self-serving = 只为自己谋利

self-starter
/ˌself ˈstɑːtə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：积极主动的人（不需督促就能做事），常见于招聘广告；（旧）（汽车的）自动启动器。
① The company is looking for a self-starter who can work without much supervision.
= 那家公司在找一个不太需要监督、积极主动的人。
a self-starter = 积极主动的人
② She is a real self-starter who set up her own business at the age of nineteen.
= 她真是个积极主动的人，十九岁就自己创业了。
a real self-starter = 真正积极主动的人

self-storage
/ˌself ˈstɔːrɪdʒ/
核心：名词，不可数：自助仓储：租用小仓库存放个人物品。
① We put our furniture into self-storage while we were living abroad for a year.
= 我们在国外住一年期间，把家具放进了自助仓库。
self-storage = 自助仓储
② A new self-storage building has opened on the edge of town with hundreds of small units.
= 镇边新开了一座自助仓储楼，有几百个小隔间。
a self-storage building = 自助仓储楼

self-study
/ˌself ˈstʌdi/
核心：名词，不可数：自学：不靠老师自己学习。作定语：self-study materials。
① My grandfather learned three languages through self-study, using old books and the radio.
= 我祖父靠旧书和收音机自学了三门语言。
through self-study = 靠自学
self-study = 自学
② The course comes with self-study materials so that students can practise at home.
= 那门课程附有自学材料，让学生可以在家练习。
self-study materials = 自学材料

self-styled
/ˌself ˈstaɪld/
核心：形容词，只作定语（常含贬义）：自封的、自称的：a self-styled expert。
① A self-styled expert on the radio said that the earthquake had been caused by the moon.
= 电台里一个自封的专家说那场地震是月亮引起的。
a self-styled expert = 自封的专家
self-styled = 自封的
② The self-styled king of rock and roll had only ever sold a few hundred records.
= 那个自称摇滚之王的人一共只卖出过几百张唱片。
the self-styled king = 自称的「之王」

self-sufficiency
/ˌself səˈfɪʃnsi/
核心：名词，不可数：自给自足、自立：不依赖外部供给。
① The farm aims at complete self-sufficiency, growing all its own food and making its own electricity.
= 那座农场以完全自给自足为目标，粮食全靠自己种，电也自己发。
complete self-sufficiency = 完全自给自足
self-sufficiency = 自给自足
② The government wants the country to reach self-sufficiency in energy within twenty years.
= 政府希望国家在二十年内实现能源自给。
self-sufficiency in something = 某方面的自给自足
用于国家时多指粮食、能源不依赖进口。

self-sufficient
/ˌself səˈfɪʃnt/
核心：形容词：自给自足的：be self-sufficient in something；（人）自立的、不依赖别人的。
① The island is self-sufficient in vegetables, but it has to bring in meat and fuel by boat.
= 那座岛蔬菜能自给自足，但肉和燃料得靠船运进来。
be self-sufficient in something = 某方面自给自足
self-sufficient = 自给自足的
② Living alone in the mountains made her very self-sufficient and good at fixing things.
= 独自住在山里让她变得非常自立，也很会修东西。
very self-sufficient = 非常自立
说人时有时略带「不需要别人、难以亲近」的意味。

self-supporting
/ˌself səˈpɔːtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：（人、机构）自立的、自负盈亏的：不靠别人资助；（建筑结构）自承重的。
① The small theatre is now self-supporting and no longer needs money from the council.
= 那家小剧院如今自负盈亏，不再需要市政拨款了。
self-supporting = 自负盈亏的
② The tent has a self-supporting frame, so you do not need to tie it to the ground.
= 那顶帐篷有自承重的支架，所以不用把它系在地上。
a self-supporting frame = 自承重的支架
说人时指经济上自立，比如学生边工边读、不靠父母。

self-sustaining
/ˌself səˈsteɪnɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我维持的、能自行持续的：a self-sustaining economy、self-sustaining growth。
① The project will be self-sustaining once the village shop starts making a profit.
= 一旦村里的商店开始盈利，那个项目就能自我维持了。
self-sustaining = 自我维持的
② Experts hope the new industry will lead to self-sustaining growth in the region.
= 专家希望新产业能带动那个地区实现可持续的自主增长。
self-sustaining growth = 可持续的自主增长
多用于经济、生态、项目，强调不再需要外部投入。

self-taught
/ˌself ˈtɔːt/
核心：形容词：自学的、自学成才的：a self-taught artist。
① He is a self-taught artist who never went to art school but sells his paintings all over the world.
= 他是个自学成才的画家，从没上过美术学校，画却卖到了世界各地。
a self-taught artist = 自学成才的画家
self-taught = 自学的
② My mother is a self-taught cook who learned everything from her grandmother.
= 我妈妈做饭是自学的，全是跟她祖母学的。
a self-taught cook = 自学的厨师
常带赞许，强调没有正式训练也有真本事。

self-timer
/ˌself ˈtaɪmə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：（相机的）自拍定时器、自动定时拍摄装置。
① We put the camera on a wall and used the self-timer so that the whole family could be in the photo.
= 我们把相机放在墙上，用自拍定时器，好让全家人都拍进照片里。
the self-timer = 自拍定时器
② Set the self-timer for ten seconds and then run and stand next to the others.
= 把自拍定时设成十秒，然后跑过去站到别人旁边。
set the self-timer = 设定自拍定时
手机相机也有这个功能，常在设置里叫「定时」。

self-titled
/ˌself ˈtaɪtld/
核心：形容词：（专辑、作品）以作者或乐队名字命名的、同名的。
① The band's first album was self-titled, simply using the name of the group.
= 那支乐队的首张专辑是同名专辑，直接用了乐队的名字。
self-titled = 同名的
② The young singer's self-titled debut sold a million copies in its first year.
= 那位年轻歌手的同名首张专辑第一年就卖了一百万张。
a self-titled debut = 同名首作
多用于音乐专辑，也用于以主角名命名的电视节目。

self-willed
/ˌself ˈwɪld/
核心：形容词（常含贬义）：任性的、固执己见的。
① She was a self-willed child who always did exactly what she wanted, whatever her parents said.
= 她是个任性的孩子，不管父母说什么，总是想干什么就干什么。
a self-willed child = 任性的孩子
self-willed = 任性的
② The self-willed old man refused to move out of his house, even when it began to fall down.
= 那个固执的老人哪怕房子开始塌了也不肯搬出去。
self-willed = 固执己见的
比 stubborn 更偏「凡事只按自己意愿」。

self-worth
/ˌself ˈwɜːθ/
核心：名词，不可数：自我价值感、自尊。
① Losing her job hurt her sense of self-worth, and she stopped going out with friends.
= 失业伤了她的自我价值感，她不再跟朋友出去了。
a sense of self-worth = 自我价值感
self-worth = 自我价值
② Volunteering at the hospital gave him a new feeling of self-worth after he retired.
= 退休后在医院做志愿者，让他重新找到了自身的价值。
a feeling of self-worth = 自我价值感
多和 sense、feeling 连用，心理学和日常都常见。

selfhood
/ˈselfhʊd/
核心：名词，不可数（正式）：自我、个性、自我意识。
① The poet writes about the struggle to find selfhood in a world that wants everyone to be the same.
= 那位诗人写的是在一个要求人人一样的世界里寻找自我的挣扎。
find selfhood = 寻找自我
selfhood = 自我
② For many young women in the novel, marriage means the loss of their selfhood.
= 对小说里许多年轻女子来说，结婚意味着失去自我。
the loss of selfhood = 失去自我
多见于文学、哲学写作，日常一般说 identity。

selfie
/ˈselfi/
核心：名词，可数（口语）：自拍照：take a selfie。
① The tourists stopped on the bridge to take a selfie with the castle behind them.
= 游客们在桥上停下，以城堡为背景自拍。
take a selfie = 自拍
a selfie = 自拍照
② Selfie sticks are banned in the museum because people kept hitting the paintings with them.
= 那座博物馆禁止使用自拍杆，因为总有人用它碰到画。
a selfie stick = 自拍杆
二〇一三年前后流行开来，现在已是日常词。

selfish
/ˈselfɪʃ/
核心：形容词：自私的、只顾自己的。副词 selfishly 是自私地。名词 selfishness 另立条。
① It was selfish of him to take the last piece of cake without asking anyone else.
= 他谁都不问就拿走最后一块蛋糕，真自私。
it is selfish of somebody to do something = 某人做某事很自私
selfish = 自私的
② She selfishly kept all the money for herself and gave nothing to her brother.
= 她自私地把钱全留给自己，一分都没给她弟弟。
selfishly = 自私地
常用 it is selfish of somebody to…句型批评某个具体行为。

selfishness
/ˈselfɪʃnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：自私。
① His selfishness ruined the holiday, because he always wanted to do only what he liked.
= 他的自私毁了那次度假，因为他总是只想做自己喜欢的事。
selfishness = 自私
② The teacher spoke to the class about selfishness after some children refused to share the toys.
= 有些孩子不肯分享玩具，老师就给全班讲了自私的问题。
talk about selfishness = 谈论自私
和它相对的是 generosity（慷慨）。

selfless
/ˈselfləs/
核心：形容词：无私的、忘我的：a selfless act。副词 selflessly 是无私地。名词 selflessness 另立条。
① The nurse was praised for her selfless work during the long winter of the epidemic.
= 那位护士因在疫情漫长的冬天里忘我工作而受到表彰。
selfless work = 忘我的工作
selfless = 无私的
② He selflessly gave up his seat on the last boat so that a young mother could leave.
= 他无私地让出了最后一条船上的座位，好让一位年轻母亲离开。
selflessly = 无私地
常带赞美，用于描述为他人做出的牺牲。

selflessness
/ˈselfləsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：无私、忘我。
① The award recognizes the selflessness of people who give their time to help others.
= 那个奖项表彰那些奉献时间帮助他人的人的无私精神。
the selflessness of somebody = 某人的无私
selflessness = 无私
② Her selflessness was clear to everyone who saw how she cared for her sick mother.
= 看过她怎样照顾生病母亲的人，都清楚她有多无私。
somebody's selflessness = 某人的无私
和 generosity 相近，更强调把别人放在自己前面。

selfsame
/ˈselfseɪm/
核心：形容词，只作定语（正式或文学）：完全相同的、同一个的：the selfsame day。用来强调 same。
① She was born and died on the selfsame day of the year, the first of May.
= 她出生和去世恰好是一年中的同一天：五月一日。
the selfsame day = 同一天
selfsame = 完全相同的
② The selfsame people who praised him last year are now calling for him to resign.
= 去年称赞他的正是如今要求他辞职的那批人。
the selfsame people = 同一批人
比 the same 语气更重，多见于书面和文学。

sell
/sel/
核心：动词（过去式和过去分词 sold）：卖、出售：sell something to somebody、sell somebody something、sell something for £10；（商品）有销路、卖得出：sell well；推销、使接受（想法）：sell an idea to somebody。反义是 buy（买）。
习语：sell like hot cakes 是畅销、抢手；sell yourself short 是低估自己、贬低自己。
复合词：selling point 是卖点；selling price 是售价。人是 seller（另立条）。
① We sold our old car to a young couple who lived in the next street.
= 我们把旧车卖给了住在隔壁街的一对年轻夫妇。
sell = 卖
sell something to somebody = 把某物卖给某人
近义对照：
buy = 买
② The little baker on the corner sells fresh bread every morning from six o'clock.
= 街角那家小面包店每天早上六点开始卖新鲜面包。
sell something = 卖某物
③ Her first novel sold well, and it was translated into twenty languages.
= 她的第一部小说卖得很好，被译成了二十种语言。
sell well = 卖得好
④ He managed to sell the idea of a four-day week to the rest of the board.
= 他成功说服董事会其他成员接受每周工作四天的想法。
sell an idea to somebody = 使某人接受某个想法
⑤ The new phones are selling like hot cakes, and most shops have run out.
= 新手机卖得像热蛋糕一样抢手，大多数商店都卖断货了。
sell like hot cakes = 畅销
⑥ Don't sell yourself short at the interview; tell them about everything you have done.
= 面试时别低估自己，把你做过的事都告诉他们。
sell yourself short = 低估自己
⑦ The company sold off its old factories in the north to pay its debts to the bank.
= 那家公司变卖了北方的旧工厂来偿还银行的债务。
sell off = 变卖
⑧ He bought the paintings cheaply and sold them on to collectors in London.
= 他低价买进那些画，再转卖给伦敦的收藏家。
sell on = 转卖
⑨ The tickets for the band's farewell concert sold out in less than ten minutes.
= 那支乐队告别演唱会的票不到十分钟就卖光了。
sell out = 卖光
⑩ Fans accused the band of selling out when they started making pop songs for adverts.
= 那支乐队开始给广告写流行歌，歌迷指责他们为钱背叛了初心。
sell out = 背叛原则
⑪ When my grandparents retired, they sold up and moved to a small town in Spain.
= 我祖父母退休时卖掉了全部家当，搬到了西班牙的一个小镇。
sell up = 卖掉全部家产
⑫ The shop measures the sell-through of each product to decide what to order next season.
= 那家店统计每种产品的售出率，决定下一季进什么货。
sell-through = 售出率
⑬ The main selling point of the flat is its view of the river.
= 那套公寓的主要卖点是能看到河景。
a selling point = 卖点
⑭ The selling price of the house was much higher than the price they had paid ten years before.
= 那座房子的售价比他们十年前买的时候高得多。
the selling price = 售价

seller
/ˈselə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：卖方、卖主、销售者；（商品）销路……的东西：a best seller、a good seller。
① The seller agreed to lower the price because the house had been on the market for a year.
= 房子挂牌一年了，卖主同意降价。
the seller = 卖方
② This toy is our best seller this Christmas, and we have almost none left.
= 这款玩具是我们今年圣诞节最畅销的，几乎卖光了。
a best seller = 畅销品
在市场上 buyers and sellers（买方与卖方）常成对说。

seltzer
/ˈseltsə(r)/
核心：名词，不可数或可数（北美）：苏打水、气泡矿泉水。
① She ordered a glass of seltzer with lime because she was driving home.
= 她要开车回家，所以点了一杯加青柠的苏打水。
seltzer = 苏打水
② My grandfather drank a cold seltzer every morning after breakfast to help his stomach.
= 我祖父每天早饭后都喝一杯冰苏打水养胃。
a seltzer = 一杯苏打水
英式多说 soda water 或 sparkling water。

selvedge
/ˈselvɪdʒ/
核心：名词，可数：（布的）织边、布边：防止脱线的边。美式也拼作 selvage。
① Cut the fabric along the selvedge so that the edge does not fray.
= 沿着布边裁布，这样边就不会脱线。
the selvedge = 布边
② Good jeans have a special selvedge along the inside of each leg.
= 好的牛仔裤每条裤腿内侧都有一道特别的布边。
a special selvedge = 特别的布边
裁缝和牛仔裤爱好者常用这个词。

semantic
/sɪˈmæntɪk/
核心：形容词：语义的、词义的：semantic change。副词 semantically 是语义上。名词 semantics 另立条。
① The two words look similar, but there is an important semantic difference between them.
= 这两个词看着相似，但语义上有重要的差别。
a semantic difference = 语义差别
semantic = 语义的
② The teacher explained that the sentence was grammatically correct but semantically very strange.
= 老师解释说，那个句子语法正确，但语义上非常怪。
semantically = 语义上
③ Words like "red", "blue" and "green" belong to the same semantic field of colour.
= 「红」「蓝」「绿」这样的词属于颜色这个语义场。
a semantic field = 语义场

semantics
/sɪˈmæntɪks/
核心：名词，不可数：语义学；（单词、句子的）含义、意思；（贬义）咬文嚼字。形式是复数，当单数用。
① She studies semantics at university and is interested in how words change their meaning.
= 她在大学研究语义学，对词义怎样变化感兴趣。
semantics = 语义学
② Whether we call it a tax or a charge is just semantics; we still have to pay it.
= 叫它税也好、费也好，不过是咬文嚼字，我们照样得付。
just semantics = 只是咬文嚼字
贬义用法常说 it's just semantics，表示争论只在用词上。

semaphore
/ˈseməfɔː(r)/
核心：名词，不可数：旗语：手持两面旗、靠手臂位置表示字母的通信方式；（铁路的）臂板信号机。动词：打旗语。
① The scouts learned to send messages in semaphore with two red and yellow flags.
= 童子军学会了用两面红黄旗打旗语传递信息。
in semaphore = 用旗语
semaphore = 旗语
② Before radio, ships used semaphore to talk to each other at sea.
= 有无线电之前，船只在海上用旗语彼此通话。
use semaphore = 用旗语
现在多见于历史和童子军活动。

semblance
/ˈsembləns/
核心：名词，单数或不可数（正式）：一点点、表面的样子：a semblance of order / normality。
① After the storm, it took weeks to bring back some semblance of normal life to the village.
= 暴风雨过后，花了好几个星期才让村子恢复一点正常生活的样子。
a semblance of something = 表面的样子、一点点
semblance = 表面的样子
② The teacher tried to keep some semblance of order in the noisy classroom.
= 老师设法在吵闹的教室里维持一点秩序。
a semblance of order = 一点秩序
几乎总用 some / a semblance of，正式书面语。

semen
/ˈsiːmən/
核心：名词，不可数：精液。
① The test checks the quality of a man's semen when a couple has trouble having children.
= 夫妇生育有困难时，这项检查用于检测男方精液的质量。
semen = 精液
② Cattle farmers can buy semen from prize bulls to improve the quality of their herds.
= 养牛的农场主可以买获奖公牛的精液来提高牛群的品质。
the semen of an animal = 动物的精液
医学、畜牧用语，日常少说。

semester
/sɪˈmestə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（尤北美）：学期：一学年分两个学期之一。英式多说 term。
① At college I am taking four courses this semester, including history and Spanish.
= 我这学期在大学选了四门课，包括历史和西班牙语。
this semester = 这学期
a semester = 学期
② She spent a semester studying in Japan as part of her university course.
= 作为大学课程的一部分，她到日本学习了一个学期。
spend a semester = 度过一个学期
英式大学一年多分三个 term，美式多分两个 semester。

semi
/ˈsemi/
核心：名词，可数（口语）：（英式）半独立式住宅：semi-detached house 的简称；（北美）半挂车；（体育）半决赛。复数 semis。
① They live in a three-bedroom semi on the edge of town, with a small garden at the back.
= 他们住在镇边一套三居室的半独立式住宅里，后面有个小花园。
a semi = 半独立式住宅
② A huge semi carrying cars was stuck under the low bridge for hours.
= 一辆运汽车的大半挂车卡在那座矮桥底下好几个小时。
a semi = （北美）半挂车
③ Our team lost in the semis, but it was still our best season for twenty years.
= 我们队在半决赛输了，可那仍是我们二十年来最好的赛季。
the semis = 半决赛

semi-annual
/ˌsemi ˈænjuəl/
核心：形容词：半年一次的、一年两次的：a semi-annual report。
① The company publishes a semi-annual report on its sales in January and July.
= 那家公司每年一月和七月发布一次半年度销售报告。
a semi-annual report = 半年度报告
semi-annual = 半年一次的
② The club holds a semi-annual meeting where members vote on important changes.
= 那个俱乐部一年开两次会，会员在会上对重要变动投票。
a semi-annual meeting = 一年两次的会议
和 biannual 同义；biennial 则是两年一次，别弄混。

semi-automatic
/ˌsemi ˌɔːtəˈmætɪk/
核心：形容词：（枪）半自动的：每扣一次扳机发射一发；（机器）半自动的。名词，可数：半自动武器。
① The police found a semi-automatic pistol hidden under the floor of the flat.
= 警察在那套公寓的地板下发现了一把半自动手枪。
a semi-automatic pistol = 半自动手枪
semi-automatic = 半自动的
② The factory uses semi-automatic machines that still need a worker to load them.
= 那家工厂用半自动机器，仍需要工人上料。
semi-automatic machines = 半自动机器
和全自动（automatic）相对，仍需人工参与。

semi-detached
/ˌsemi dɪˈtætʃt/
核心：形容词（英式）：（房屋）半独立式的：与另一栋共用一面墙。名词（口语）简称 semi。
① Last year we bought a semi-detached house with three bedrooms and a garage.
= 去年我们买了一套带三间卧室和车库的半独立式住宅。
a semi-detached house = 半独立式住宅
semi-detached = 半独立式的
② The quiet street is full of red brick semi-detached houses built in the 1930s.
= 那条安静的街上满是二十世纪三十年代建的红砖半独立式住宅。
semi-detached houses = 半独立式住宅
与 detached（独栋）、terraced（联排）并列，是英国常见的三种房型。

semi-final
/ˌsemi ˈfaɪnl/
核心：名词，可数：半决赛。常用复数 the semi-finals。
① Our team reached the semi-final for the first time in fifty years.
= 我们队五十年来第一次打进了半决赛。
reach the semi-final = 打进半决赛
a semi-final = 半决赛
② She lost in the semi-finals to the player who went on to win the whole tournament.
= 她在半决赛输给了后来夺得整个锦标赛冠军的选手。
lose in the semi-finals = 在半决赛中落败
美式也写作 semifinal。

semi-finalist
/ˌsemi ˈfaɪnəlɪst/
核心：名词，可数：半决赛选手、进入半决赛的队伍。
① The four semi-finalists will play on Saturday, and the final is next Sunday.
= 四名半决赛选手星期六比赛，决赛在下个星期天。
semi-finalists = 半决赛选手
② At fifteen, she was the youngest semi-finalist in the history of the competition.
= 她十五岁时成了那项比赛历史上最年轻的半决赛选手。
the youngest semi-finalist = 最年轻的半决赛选手
同理，进入决赛的叫 finalist。

semi-precious
/ˌsemi ˈpreʃəs/
核心：形容词：（宝石）次贵重的、半宝石的：如紫水晶、绿松石。
① At the wedding she wore a silver necklace set with small blue semi-precious stones.
= 婚礼上她戴着一条镶小颗蓝色半宝石的银项链。
semi-precious stones = 半宝石
semi-precious = 次贵重的
② Turquoise and amber are semi-precious, while diamonds and rubies are precious stones.
= 绿松石和琥珀是半宝石，而钻石和红宝石是贵重宝石。
be semi-precious = 是半宝石
与 precious stones（贵重宝石）相对。

semi-professional
/ˌsemi prəˈfeʃənl/
核心：形容词：半职业的：（运动员、音乐家）以此为副业、领一部分报酬。名词，可数：半职业选手。
① He plays football for a semi-professional team and works in a bank during the week.
= 他在一支半职业球队踢球，平时在银行上班。
a semi-professional team = 半职业球队
semi-professional = 半职业的
② Most of the musicians in the orchestra are semi-professionals with other jobs.
= 那个乐团的大多数乐手都是另有工作的半职业乐手。
semi-professionals = 半职业选手
口语常简称 semi-pro。

semi-skilled
/ˌsemi ˈskɪld/
核心：形容词：（工人、工作）半熟练的：需要一些训练但不多。
① The factory employs mostly semi-skilled workers who are trained on the job.
= 那家工厂雇的大多是在岗培训的半熟练工人。
semi-skilled workers = 半熟练工人
semi-skilled = 半熟练的
② Many semi-skilled jobs in the factory disappeared when the new machines arrived.
= 新机器一来，工厂里许多半熟练的工作就消失了。
semi-skilled jobs = 半熟练的工作
介于 skilled（熟练）和 unskilled（非熟练）之间。

semi-skimmed
/ˌsemi ˈskɪmd/
核心：形容词（英式）：（牛奶）半脱脂的。
① I always buy semi-skimmed milk because it has less fat than full-fat milk.
= 我总买半脱脂牛奶，因为它比全脂奶脂肪少。
semi-skimmed milk = 半脱脂牛奶
semi-skimmed = 半脱脂的
② Could I have a large coffee with semi-skimmed milk and no sugar, please?
= 请给我一大杯咖啡，加半脱脂奶，不要糖，好吗？
semi-skimmed = 半脱脂奶（口语省略 milk）
英国超市牛奶常分 whole、semi-skimmed、skimmed 三种。

semi-sweet
/ˌsemi ˈswiːt/
核心：形容词：（巧克力、葡萄酒）半甜的、微甜的。
① The recipe uses semi-sweet chocolate, which is less sweet than milk chocolate.
= 那个食谱用的是半甜巧克力，比牛奶巧克力甜度低。
semi-sweet chocolate = 半甜巧克力
semi-sweet = 半甜的
② We had a bottle of semi-sweet white wine with the fruit dessert.
= 我们配水果甜点喝了一瓶半甜白葡萄酒。
semi-sweet wine = 半甜葡萄酒
介于 sweet（甜）和 dry（干）之间。

semi-trailer
/ˌsemi ˈtreɪlə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（北美）：半挂车：前部搭在牵引车上的大型挂车。口语简称 semi。
① A semi-trailer carrying fruit turned over on the motorway and closed two lanes.
= 一辆运水果的半挂车在高速公路上翻了，封了两条车道。
a semi-trailer = 半挂车
② The driver parked his semi-trailer at the truck stop and went to sleep in the cab.
= 司机把半挂车停在卡车休息站，到驾驶室里睡觉。
park a semi-trailer = 停半挂车
英式多说 articulated lorry。

semi-tropical
/ˌsemi ˈtrɒpɪkl/
核心：形容词：亚热带的。同 subtropical。
① The garden is full of semi-tropical plants that grow well in the warm, wet climate.
= 那个花园满是亚热带植物，在温暖潮湿的气候里长得很好。
semi-tropical plants = 亚热带植物
semi-tropical = 亚热带的
② The southern part of the country has a semi-tropical climate with mild winters.
= 那个国家的南部是亚热带气候，冬天温和。
a semi-tropical climate = 亚热带气候
比 subtropical 口语一些。

semi-weekly
/ˌsemi ˈwiːkli/
核心：形容词、副词：每周两次的、每周两次地。
① The little village by the river has a semi-weekly market on Tuesdays and Fridays.
= 河边那个小村子每周有两次集市，在星期二和星期五。
a semi-weekly market = 每周两次的集市
semi-weekly = 每周两次的
② The local newspaper was published semi-weekly for years before it became a daily paper.
= 那份地方报纸多年来每周出两期，后来才改成日报。
be published semi-weekly = 每周出两次
易与 biweekly（两周一次或每周两次，有歧义）混淆。

semibreve
/ˈsemibriːv/
核心：名词，可数（英式，音乐）：全音符：时值最长的常用音符，美式说 whole note。
① In our first music lesson we learned that a semibreve lasts as long as four crotchets.
= 第一节音乐课上我们学到，一个全音符的时值等于四个四分音符。
a semibreve = 全音符
② The music teacher drew a semibreve on the board and asked us to count four beats.
= 音乐老师在黑板上画了一个全音符，让我们数四拍。
draw a semibreve = 画全音符
英式乐理用 semibreve、minim、crotchet、quaver 这一套名称。

semicircle
/ˈsemisɜːkl/
核心：名词，可数：半圆；半圆形：in a semicircle。
① The children sat in a semicircle on the floor around the teacher while she read the story.
= 老师讲故事时，孩子们围着她在地上坐成半圆。
in a semicircle = 成半圆形
a semicircle = 半圆
② Draw a semicircle above the line, and then add a smaller one inside it.
= 在那条线上方画一个半圆，再在里面加一个小一点的。
draw a semicircle = 画半圆
常说 sit / stand in a semicircle 表示围坐成半圆。

semicircular
/ˌsemiˈsɜːkjələ(r)/
核心：形容词：半圆形的：a semicircular window。
① The old church has a semicircular window above the door with coloured glass.
= 那座老教堂的门上方有一扇镶彩色玻璃的半圆形窗户。
a semicircular window = 半圆形窗户
semicircular = 半圆形的
② The theatre has semicircular rows of seats, so everyone has a good view of the stage.
= 那座剧场的座位排成半圆形，所以每个人都能看清舞台。
semicircular rows = 半圆形的座位排
解剖学里 semicircular canals 是内耳的半规管。

semicolon
/ˌsemiˈkəʊlən/
核心：名词，可数：分号（;）：用来连接两个关系紧密的独立分句，或分隔较长的列举项。
① Use a semicolon to join two sentences that are closely connected in meaning.
= 用分号连接两个意思紧密相关的句子。
a semicolon = 分号
② The teacher said there were too many semicolons in my essay and told me to use full stops instead.
= 老师说我作文里分号太多，叫我改用句号。
too many semicolons = 分号太多
比逗号停顿长、比句号短。

semiconductor
/ˌsemikənˈdʌktə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：半导体：导电性介于导体和绝缘体之间的材料，如硅，用来做电子元件；半导体器件、芯片。
① The engineer explained that silicon is the most important semiconductor used in computer chips.
= 工程师解释说，硅是电脑芯片中最重要的半导体材料。
a semiconductor = 半导体
② The country is building new factories to make semiconductors for phones and cars.
= 那个国家正在建新工厂，为手机和汽车生产半导体芯片。
make semiconductors = 生产半导体芯片
新闻里常说 semiconductor industry（半导体产业）。

seminal
/ˈsemɪnl/
核心：形容词：（作品、思想）有深远影响的、开创性的：a seminal work；（医学）精液的。
① Her seminal book on climate change changed the way governments thought about the problem.
= 她那本关于气候变化的开创性著作改变了各国政府对这个问题的看法。
a seminal book = 开创性著作
seminal = 开创性的
② The band's first album is seen as a seminal moment in the history of rock music.
= 那支乐队的首张专辑被视为摇滚乐史上具有里程碑意义的时刻。
a seminal moment = 具有里程碑意义的时刻
比 important、influential 更强调「开风气之先、影响后来」。

seminar
/ˈsemɪnɑː(r)/
核心：名词，可数：（大学的）研讨课、讨论课；（专题）研讨会、讲习会。
① We have a history seminar every Thursday, where ten students discuss a book with the professor.
= 我们每周四有一节历史研讨课，十个学生和教授一起讨论一本书。
a history seminar = 历史研讨课
a seminar = 研讨课
② The bank held a free seminar for small businesses on how to manage their money.
= 那家银行为小企业举办了一场免费讲座，讲怎样管理资金。
hold a seminar = 举办研讨会
和 lecture（大课、讲座）相比，seminar 人少、以讨论为主。

seminarian
/ˌsemɪˈneəriən/
核心：名词，可数：神学院学生。
① The young seminarian studied for six years before he became a priest.
= 那个年轻的神学院学生学了六年才成为神父。
a seminarian = 神学院学生
② A group of seminarians from Rome visited the old cathedral during their summer holidays.
= 一群来自罗马的神学院学生暑假期间参观了那座古老的大教堂。
seminarians = 神学院学生们
多指天主教培养神父的学校里的学生。

seminary
/ˈsemɪnəri/
核心：名词，可数：神学院：培养神父、牧师、拉比的学校；（旧）女子学校。
① He went to a seminary at eighteen because he wanted to become a priest.
= 他十八岁进了神学院，因为想当神父。
a seminary = 神学院
② The old building was once a seminary for young women and is now a hotel.
= 那栋老楼从前是一所女子学校，现在成了酒店。
a seminary for young women = 女子学校
「女子学校」一义已经过时，现在只见于旧书和建筑史。

Seminole
/ˈsemɪnəʊl/
核心：名词，可数，复数 Seminole 或 Seminoles：塞米诺尔人：美国佛罗里达、俄克拉何马的原住民族。形容词：塞米诺尔人的。
① The Seminole lived in the swamps of Florida and fought for many years to keep their land.
= 塞米诺尔人住在佛罗里达的沼泽地里，为保住土地奋战了多年。
the Seminole = 塞米诺尔人
② The museum has a collection of colourful Seminole clothing made by hand.
= 那座博物馆收藏了一批手工制作的、色彩鲜艳的塞米诺尔服饰。
Seminole clothing = 塞米诺尔服饰
作专名，首字母总是大写。

semiotic
/ˌsemiˈɒtɪk/
核心：形容词：符号学的；符号的。名词 semiotics 另立条。
① The professor gave a semiotic reading of the advertisement, explaining what each colour and image meant.
= 那位教授对那则广告做了符号学解读，讲解每种颜色、每个图像的含义。
a semiotic reading = 符号学解读
semiotic = 符号学的
② Traffic lights are a simple semiotic system that drivers in every country understand.
= 交通信号灯是一套各国司机都懂的简单符号系统。
a semiotic system = 符号系统
学术用语，日常少说。

semiotics
/ˌsemiˈɒtɪks/
核心：名词，不可数：符号学：研究符号、标志及其意义的学科。形式是复数，当单数用。
① She studied semiotics and wrote her thesis on the meaning of colours in films.
= 她学的是符号学，论文写的是电影中颜色的含义。
semiotics = 符号学
② Semiotics looks at how signs, from road signs to fashion, communicate meaning.
= 符号学研究从路标到时装的各种符号如何传达意义。
semiotics looks at something = 符号学研究某事
常用于文学、传媒、广告研究。

semiquaver
/ˈsemikweɪvə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（英式，音乐）：十六分音符，美式说 sixteenth note。
① The fast part of the piece is full of semiquavers, so it is hard to play well.
= 那首曲子的快板部分满是十六分音符，很难弹好。
semiquavers = 十六分音符
② The piano teacher showed me that four semiquavers have the same length as one crotchet.
= 钢琴老师给我演示，四个十六分音符的时值等于一个四分音符。
four semiquavers = 四个十六分音符
时值是 quaver（八分音符）的一半。

Semite
/ˈsiːmaɪt/
核心：名词，可数：闪米特人：包括古今犹太人、阿拉伯人等说闪米特语的民族。
① The ancient Hebrews and Phoenicians were Semites who spoke closely related languages.
= 古希伯来人和腓尼基人都是闪米特人，说的语言彼此密切相关。
Semites = 闪米特人
② Thousands of years ago, Semites from the desert settled in the river valleys and built the first cities there.
= 几千年前，来自沙漠的闪米特人在河谷定居，在那里建起了最早的城市。
Semites from the desert = 来自沙漠的闪米特人
今天多见于历史、语言学语境。

Semitic
/səˈmɪtɪk/
核心：形容词：闪米特语族的：Semitic languages（希伯来语、阿拉伯语等）；闪米特人的；犹太人的。
① Hebrew and Arabic are both Semitic languages, so they share many words and sounds.
= 希伯来语和阿拉伯语都是闪米特语言，所以有许多相同的词和音。
Semitic languages = 闪米特语族
Semitic = 闪米特语族的
② The museum has a room of Semitic objects from the ancient cities of the Middle East.
= 那座博物馆有一间展厅，陈列着中东古城的闪米特文物。
Semitic objects = 闪米特文物
说语言时最常用；作「犹太人的」讲时多见于固定说法。

semitone
/ˈsemitəʊn/
核心：名词，可数（英式，音乐）：半音：相邻两个琴键之间的音程，美式说 half step。
① Play the note and then the one a semitone higher, using the black key next to it.
= 先弹这个音，再用旁边的黑键弹高半音的那个音。
a semitone higher = 高半音
a semitone = 半音
② The singer asked the band to play the song a semitone lower because it was too high for her.
= 那首歌对她来说太高了，歌手请乐队把调降低半音。
a semitone lower = 低半音
两个半音等于一个全音（tone）。

semivowel
/ˈsemivaʊəl/
核心：名词，可数（语音）：半元音：如 /w/ 和 /j/，发音像元音、作用像辅音。
① In English, the sounds at the start of "wet" and "yes" are semivowels.
= 在英语里，「wet」和「yes」开头的音是半元音。
semivowels = 半元音
a semivowel = 半元音
② Learners often find English semivowels easy, because their own language has similar sounds.
= 学习者常觉得英语的半元音容易，因为他们的母语里有类似的音。
English semivowels = 英语半元音
也叫 glide（滑音）。

semolina
/ˌseməˈliːnə/
核心：名词，不可数：粗粒小麦粉、粗面粉：做意大利面、布丁用。
① My grandmother made sweet semolina pudding with milk and served it with jam.
= 我祖母用牛奶做甜的粗麦粉布丁，配果酱吃。
semolina pudding = 粗麦粉布丁
semolina = 粗粒小麦粉
② In Italy, good pasta is usually made from hard wheat semolina and water.
= 在意大利，好的意大利面通常是用硬质小麦粗粉加水做成的。
hard wheat semolina = 硬质小麦粗粉
英国人常把 semolina pudding 和学校食堂联系在一起。

Sen.
/ˈsenətə(r)/
核心：缩写：参议员（Senator 的缩写，用在姓名前）；（较少）年长的（Senior 的缩写，同 Sr）。照 Rep.（众议员）的写法收。
① The letter was signed by Sen. John Miller, who has represented the state for twenty years.
= 那封信是参议员约翰·米勒签的，他代表那个州已经二十年了。
Sen. = 参议员（缩写）
② The newspaper quoted Sen. Lopez, who said that the new law would help small farmers.
= 那家报纸引述了参议员洛佩斯的话，她说新法律会帮助小农户。
Sen. Lopez = 参议员洛佩斯
多见于美国报刊，读的时候读全称 Senator。

senate
/ˈsenət/
核心：名词，可数（常作 the Senate）：（美国、法国等的）参议院、上议院；（大学的）评议会、理事会；（古罗马的）元老院。
① The new law was approved by the House of Representatives but rejected by the Senate.
= 那项新法律获得众议院通过，却被参议院否决了。
the Senate = 参议院
② Last month the university senate voted to change the rules for final exams.
= 上个月大学评议会投票决定修改期末考试规则。
the university senate = 大学评议会
③ In ancient Rome, the senate was made up of the most powerful families in the city.
= 在古罗马，元老院由城中最有权势的家族组成。
the senate of ancient Rome = 古罗马元老院

senator
/ˈsenətə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（常大写 Senator）：参议员；（古罗马的）元老院议员。缩写 Sen.。
① The senator for California spoke against the plan in a long speech.
= 加利福尼亚州的参议员发表长篇演讲反对那项计划。
a senator = 参议员
② She was elected to the Senate at thirty-five and became the youngest senator in the country.
= 她三十五岁当选参议员，成了全国最年轻的参议员。
the youngest senator = 最年轻的参议员
美国每州两名参议员，任期六年。

senatorial
/ˌsenəˈtɔːriəl/
核心：形容词：参议员的、参议院的：a senatorial election。
① The senatorial election in the state is expected to be very close this year.
= 预计今年该州的参议员选举会非常激烈。
a senatorial election = 参议员选举
senatorial = 参议员的
② At the dinner he spoke in a slow, senatorial voice that made everyone at the table listen.
= 晚宴上，他用一种缓慢庄重、参议员般的语调说话，桌上每个人都在听。
a senatorial voice = 参议员般庄重的语调
引申义形容人说话庄重、有派头。

send
/send/
核心：动词（过去式和过去分词 sent）：寄、发送：send a letter、send somebody something、send something to somebody；派、打发（某人去某处）：send somebody to bed、send for the doctor；使处于某种状态：send somebody to sleep、send prices up。
① My grandmother sent me a long letter for my birthday with photos of the family.
= 我祖母为我的生日寄来一封长信，附了全家的照片。
send somebody something = 给某人发某物
send = 寄、发送
② I sent the documents to the bank by email, but they say they have not received them.
= 我用电子邮件把文件发给了银行，可他们说没收到。
sent = 发送了（send 的过去式）
③ The children were sent to bed early because they had been fighting all afternoon.
= 孩子们打了一下午架，被早早打发去睡觉了。
send somebody to bed = 打发某人去睡觉
④ The mayor's long and boring speech nearly sent the whole audience to sleep.
= 市长那篇又长又乏味的讲话差点让全体听众睡着。
send somebody to sleep = 使某人睡着
⑤ The guard sent the reporters away and closed the gates of the palace.
= 卫兵把记者们打发走，关上了宫门。
send away = 打发走
⑥ The judge sent him down for five years for robbing the post office.
= 法官因他抢劫邮局判了他五年监禁。
send down = 把……关进监狱
⑦ When the baby got a high temperature, we sent for the doctor.
= 宝宝发高烧时，我们派人去请了医生。
send for somebody = 派人去请某人
⑧ The volcano sent forth clouds of ash that covered the whole valley.
= 那座火山喷出的火山灰云覆盖了整个山谷。
send forth = 发出
⑨ The government sent in the army to help the people after the floods.
= 洪水过后，政府派军队去帮助灾民。
send in = 派遣
⑩ I sent off my application for the job on Friday and got a reply on Monday.
= 我星期五寄出了求职申请，星期一就收到了回复。
send off = 寄出
⑪ The referee sent off two players for fighting in the second half.
= 下半场裁判把两名打架的球员罚下了场。
send somebody off = 把某人罚下场
⑫ If any letters come for me after I move, please send them on to my new address.
= 我搬走后要是有寄给我的信，请转寄到我的新地址。
send on = 转寄
⑬ The company sent out invitations to all its customers for the opening of the new shop.
= 那家公司给所有顾客发出了新店开业的请柬。
send out = 发出
⑭ We were too tired to cook, so we sent out for a pizza.
= 我们累得不想做饭，就叫了一份比萨外卖。
send out for something = 叫某物外卖
⑮ The comedy show sends up famous politicians by copying the way they speak.
= 那档喜剧节目模仿名政客说话的样子来取笑他们。
send up = 模仿取笑

sender
/ˈsendə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：寄件人、发送者。
① If nobody is at home, the parcel will be returned to the sender.
= 如果家里没人，包裹会被退回寄件人。
the sender = 寄件人
② Do not open emails if you do not know the sender, because they may contain viruses.
= 不认识发件人的邮件别打开，里面可能有病毒。
know the sender = 认识发件人
信封上常写 return to sender（退回寄件人）。

sending-off
/ˌsendɪŋ ˈɒf/
核心：名词，可数（英式，体育）：罚下场。
① The striker's sending-off in the first half meant that his team played with ten men.
= 那名前锋上半场被罚下，他的球队只能十人应战。
a sending-off = 罚下场
② There were two sending-offs and six yellow cards in a very rough match.
= 那场比赛非常粗野，有两人被罚下、六张黄牌。
two sending-offs = 两人被罚下
动词说法是 send somebody off。

Senegalese
/ˌsenɪɡəˈliːz/
核心：形容词：塞内加尔的、塞内加尔人的。名词，可数，复数也是 Senegalese：塞内加尔人。
① We listened to Senegalese music played on drums and a long wooden harp.
= 我们听了用鼓和一种长木竖琴演奏的塞内加尔音乐。
Senegalese = 塞内加尔的
② Many Senegalese work in France and send money home to their families.
= 许多塞内加尔人在法国工作，往家里寄钱。
the Senegalese = 塞内加尔人
塞内加尔讲法语，所以和法国联系紧密。

senescence
/sɪˈnesns/
核心：名词，不可数（生物）：衰老、老化。
① Scientists at the university are studying the cells to understand why senescence happens.
= 那所大学的科学家正在研究那些细胞，以弄清衰老为什么会发生。
senescence = 衰老
② In autumn, the senescence of the leaves turns them yellow and red.
= 秋天，叶子衰老，变成了黄色和红色。
the senescence of leaves = 叶子的衰老
学术用语，日常说 ageing。

senescent
/sɪˈnesnt/
核心：形容词（生物）：衰老的、老化的。
① Senescent cells stop dividing but stay in the body and can cause problems.
= 衰老的细胞停止分裂，却留在体内，可能引发问题。
senescent cells = 衰老细胞
senescent = 衰老的
② The gardener removed the senescent leaves so that the plant could grow new ones.
= 园丁摘掉了老化的叶子，好让植物长出新叶。
senescent leaves = 老化的叶子
多见于生物、医学写作。

senile
/ˈsiːnaɪl/
核心：形容词：衰老的、老糊涂的（指年老而记忆、思维衰退）。说人时有些冒犯，宜慎用。
① My grandfather became a little senile in his nineties and often forgot our names.
= 我祖父九十多岁时有点老糊涂了，常常忘记我们的名字。
become senile = 变得老糊涂
senile = 老糊涂的
② The old hospital in town has a special ward for patients with senile dementia.
= 镇上那家老医院有一个专门收治老年痴呆患者的病房。
senile dementia = 老年痴呆
医学上现在多说 dementia，不带 senile。

senility
/səˈnɪləti/
核心：名词，不可数：衰老、老年糊涂。
① The old king showed signs of senility, so his son began to rule in his place.
= 老国王显出衰老糊涂的迹象，所以他的儿子开始代他治国。
signs of senility = 衰老的迹象
senility = 衰老
② Forgetting where you put your keys is not always a sign of senility.
= 忘记钥匙放在哪儿，不一定是衰老的迹象。
a sign of senility = 衰老的迹象
日常用语，略带贬义。

senior
/ˈsiːniə(r)/
核心：形容词：（职位、级别）较高的、资深的：a senior manager；年长的（缩写 Sr、Snr）。名词，可数：年长者：be three years my senior；（北美）（高中、大学的）毕业班学生；（尤北美）老年人：senior citizens。反义 junior。
① She is a senior manager at the bank and has fifty people working for her.
= 她是那家银行的高级经理，手下有五十个人。
a senior manager = 高级经理
senior = 资深的
② My husband is five years my senior, but he looks much younger.
= 我丈夫比我大五岁，可看起来年轻得多。
be five years my senior = 比我大五岁
③ She is a senior at high school and hopes to go to university next year.
= 她是高中毕业班学生，希望明年上大学。
a senior = 毕业班学生
④ The city museum offers free entry to senior citizens and children on Mondays.
= 市博物馆星期一对老年人和儿童免费开放。
senior citizens = 老年人
⑤ I had a senior moment and put the milk in the cupboard instead of the fridge.
= 我一时糊涂，把牛奶放进了橱柜而不是冰箱。
a senior moment = 一时糊涂
⑥ My cousin goes to a senior high school in Texas and plays in the school band.
= 我表弟在得克萨斯州上高中，在学校乐队里演奏。
a senior high school = （美国）高中
⑦ The professors drink coffee and read the newspapers in the senior common room.
= 教授们在教师休息室里喝咖啡、看报纸。
the senior common room = 教师休息室
⑧ He joined the navy at eighteen and retired as a senior chief petty officer.
= 他十八岁加入海军，以高级军士长的军衔退役。
a senior chief petty officer = （海军）高级军士长
⑨ My uncle served as a senior master sergeant in the air force for twenty years.
= 我叔叔在空军当了二十年的高级军士长。
a senior master sergeant = （美国空军）高级军士长
⑩ She joined the Royal Air Force and became a senior aircraftwoman after a year.
= 她加入皇家空军，一年后成为空军一级女兵。
a senior aircraftwoman = 空军一级女兵
男兵叫 senior aircraftman。
⑪ Every senior aircraftman in the unit was trained to repair the engines.
= 部队里每个空军一级兵都受过修理发动机的训练。
a senior aircraftman = 空军一级兵

seniority
/ˌsiːniˈɒrəti/
核心：名词，不可数：资历、年资；（职位）较高的等级：by seniority。
① In this company, people are promoted by seniority rather than by how good they are.
= 在这家公司，升职看资历，而不看能力。
by seniority = 按资历
seniority = 资历
② Workers with the most seniority were the last to lose their jobs when the factory closed.
= 工厂倒闭时，资历最深的工人是最后一批丢工作的。
the most seniority = 资历最深
工会合同里常按 seniority 安排升迁和裁员顺序。

sensation
/senˈseɪʃn/
核心：名词：（身体的）感觉、知觉（可数或不可数）：a burning sensation、lose sensation；（说不清的）感觉（可数）：have the sensation that…；轰动、引起轰动的人或事（可数，常单数）：cause a sensation、an overnight sensation。形容词 sensational 另立条。
① I had a strange burning sensation in my throat after drinking the hot sauce.
= 喝了那辣酱之后，我喉咙里有一种奇怪的灼烧感。
a burning sensation = 灼烧感
sensation = 感觉
② After the accident, he lost all sensation in his left leg for several weeks.
= 事故后，他左腿有好几个星期完全没了知觉。
lose sensation = 失去知觉
③ I had the strange sensation that someone was watching me from the trees.
= 我有一种奇怪的感觉，好像有人在树林里盯着我。
have the sensation that = 有……的感觉
④ The young singer caused a sensation when she appeared on television for the first time.
= 那位年轻歌手第一次上电视就引起了轰动。
cause a sensation = 引起轰动

sensational
/senˈseɪʃənl/
核心：形容词：轰动的、耸人听闻的（常贬义，指新闻）：sensational stories；（口语）极好的、精彩的：a sensational goal。副词 sensationally 是轰动地、极其。
① The newspaper printed sensational stories about the actor's private life to sell more copies.
= 那家报纸刊登关于那位演员私生活的耸人听闻的故事，好多卖几份。
sensational stories = 耸人听闻的故事
sensational = 耸人听闻的
② He scored a sensational goal from forty yards in the last minute.
= 他在最后一分钟从四十码外打进了一记精彩绝伦的进球。
a sensational goal = 精彩的进球
③ The little village team sensationally beat the champions in the cup final last May.
= 去年五月，那支小村队在杯赛决赛中爆冷击败了冠军队。
sensationally = 轰动地

sensationalism
/senˈseɪʃənəlɪzəm/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：（新闻的）煽情手法、哗众取宠。
① The television channel was accused of sensationalism in its reports about the crime.
= 那家电视台被指在报道那起罪案时哗众取宠。
sensationalism = 哗众取宠
② Good journalists avoid sensationalism and report the facts calmly, even when the story is shocking.
= 好记者不搞煽情，即使新闻令人震惊，也冷静地报道事实。
avoid sensationalism = 不搞煽情
常用来批评小报和娱乐新闻。

sensationalist
/senˈseɪʃənəlɪst/
核心：形容词（贬义）：哗众取宠的、渲染耸人听闻的。名词，可数：哗众取宠的人。
① The sensationalist headlines in the papers frightened people more than the actual news did.
= 报纸上那些耸人听闻的标题比新闻本身更让人害怕。
sensationalist headlines = 耸人听闻的标题
sensationalist = 哗众取宠的
② He is a sensationalist who will say anything to get attention on television.
= 他是个哗众取宠的人，为了在电视上博关注什么话都说得出口。
a sensationalist = 哗众取宠的人
多修饰 press、media、headlines、reporting。

sensationalize
/senˈseɪʃənəlaɪz/
核心：动词，及物（贬义）：渲染、夸大（以引起轰动）。英式也拼作 sensationalise。
① The magazine sensationalized the story to make it seem much worse than it really was.
= 那份杂志渲染那个故事，把它说得比实际糟糕得多。
sensationalize a story = 渲染故事
sensationalize = 渲染、夸大
② The police asked the newspapers not to sensationalize the crime, out of respect for the family.
= 出于对家属的尊重，警方请报纸不要渲染那起罪案。
sensationalize a crime = 渲染罪案
宾语多是 story、news、crime。

sense
/sens/
核心：名词：感官、官能（可数）：the five senses、a sense of smell；（单数）感觉、意识：a sense of humour、a sense of direction、a sense of guilt；（不可数）判断力、道理：common sense、there is no sense in doing something；（可数）意义、含义：in a sense、in every sense；（复数 senses）理智：come to your senses。动词：（凭直觉）感觉到、意识到：sense danger、sense that…。
习语：make sense 是讲得通、有道理；in a sense 是在某种意义上；common sense 是常识、判断力；come to your senses 是醒悟过来；a sense of humour 是幽默感。
① Dogs have a much better sense of smell than people do, so they can find lost children.
= 狗的嗅觉比人灵敏得多，所以能找到走失的孩子。
a sense of smell = 嗅觉
② Being part of the team gave him a sense of belonging for the first time in his life.
= 加入团队让他平生第一次有了归属感。
a sense of = …感
③ The instructions do not make sense; I have read them three times and still do not understand.
= 这说明书讲不通，我读了三遍还是不明白。
make sense = 讲得通
④ In a sense, losing the job was the best thing that ever happened to her.
= 从某种意义上说，丢了那份工作是她遇到的最好的事。
in a sense = 在某种意义上
⑤ Use your common sense and take an umbrella if the sky looks grey.
= 动动脑子，天色发灰就带把伞。
common sense = 常识、判断力
⑥ He was going to give up university, but he came to his senses and finished his degree.
= 他本来打算退学，可后来醒悟过来，读完了学位。
come to your senses = 醒悟过来
⑦ My father has a great sense of humour and can make anyone laugh.
= 我父亲很有幽默感，能把谁都逗笑。
a sense of humour = 幽默感
⑧ There is no sense in waiting any longer; the last bus has already gone.
= 再等也没有意义了，末班车已经走了。
there is no sense in doing something = 做某事没有意义
⑨ The horse sensed danger and refused to go any further along the path.
= 那匹马察觉到危险，不肯再沿着小路往前走。
sense danger = 察觉到危险
⑩ She sensed that something was wrong as soon as she walked into the house.
= 她一进屋就感觉到有点不对劲。
sense that = 感觉到

senseless
/ˈsensləs/
核心：形容词：无意义的、愚蠢的、毫无道理的：a senseless attack；失去知觉的：be knocked senseless。副词 senselessly 是无意义地。
① The police called the attack on the old man a senseless act of violence.
= 警方称袭击那位老人是一起毫无意义的暴力行为。
a senseless act = 毫无意义的行为
senseless = 无意义的
② The boxer was knocked senseless in the third round and taken to hospital.
= 那名拳击手第三回合被打得不省人事，送进了医院。
be knocked senseless = 被打得不省人事
③ Thousands of trees were senselessly cut down to build a road that was never finished.
= 为修一条始终没修完的路，成千上万棵树被毫无意义地砍掉了。
senselessly = 无意义地

sensibility
/ˌsensəˈbɪləti/
核心：名词：（对艺术、美的）感受力、鉴赏力（不可数或可数）：artistic sensibility；（复数 sensibilities）（易受伤害的）感情、情感：offend somebody's sensibilities。
① The young painter showed great artistic sensibility in his use of colour and light.
= 那位年轻画家在用色和用光上表现出很强的艺术感受力。
artistic sensibility = 艺术感受力
sensibility = 感受力
② The film was cut so that it would not offend the sensibilities of older viewers.
= 那部电影做了删减，以免冒犯年长观众的感情。
offend somebody's sensibilities = 冒犯某人的感情
注意和 sensitivity、sensible（明智的）区分。

sensible
/ˈsensəbl/
核心：形容词：明智的、合理的、懂事的：a sensible decision、it is sensible to do something；（衣服、鞋）实用的、合适的：sensible shoes。注意和 sensitive（敏感的）区分。副词 sensibly 是明智地、合理地。
① It would be sensible to book the tickets early, because the concert always sells out.
= 早点订票是明智的，因为那场音乐会总是卖光。
sensible = 明智的
it is sensible to do something = 做某事是明智的
② Wear sensible shoes for the walk, because the path is rocky and wet.
= 徒步要穿合适的鞋，因为路上多石头又湿滑。
sensible shoes = 实用合脚的鞋
③ The children behaved very sensibly while their parents were out at the cinema on Saturday night.
= 父母出门时，孩子们表现得很懂事。
sensibly = 明智地、懂事地

sensitive
/ˈsensətɪv/
核心：形容词：（对别人的感受）体贴的、善解人意的：be sensitive to somebody's needs；（人）敏感的、易生气的：be sensitive about something；（皮肤、身体）敏感的、易受影响的：sensitive skin；（话题）敏感的、须谨慎处理的：a sensitive issue；（仪器）灵敏的。注意和 sensible（明智的）区分。副词 sensitively 是体贴地、谨慎地。名词 sensitivity 另立条。
① A good teacher is sensitive to the needs of children who are having problems at home.
= 好老师能体察家里有困难的孩子的需要。
be sensitive to = 对…敏感
sensitive = 敏感的
近义对照：
sensible = 明智的
② She is very sensitive about her weight, so please don't mention it when we have dinner with her.
= 她对自己的体重很敏感，所以别提这个。
be sensitive about something = 对某事敏感
③ The chemist told me this cream is good for people with sensitive skin who burn easily in summer.
= 这种面霜适合皮肤敏感的人用。
sensitive skin = 敏感肌肤
④ Religion is a sensitive subject, so be careful what you say at dinner.
= 宗教是个敏感话题，所以晚饭时说话要小心。
a sensitive subject = 敏感话题
⑤ In the quiet hospital room, the doctor spoke sensitively to the family about their father's illness.
= 医生体贴地跟那一家人谈了他们父亲的病情。
sensitively = 体贴地

sensitivity
/ˌsensəˈtɪvəti/
核心：名词：体贴、善解人意（不可数）；敏感（不可数或可数）：sensitivity to light；（话题的）敏感性（不可数）；（仪器的）灵敏度。
① After the accident, the nurse dealt with the frightened children in the waiting room with great sensitivity.
= 那位护士非常体贴地对待那些受惊的孩子。
with sensitivity = 体贴地
sensitivity = 体贴
② Some people have a sensitivity to bright light and need to wear dark glasses.
= 有些人对强光敏感，得戴墨镜。
a sensitivity to something = 对某物敏感
③ Because of the sensitivity of the subject, the meeting was held in private.
= 由于话题敏感，那次会议是闭门举行的。
the sensitivity of a subject = 话题的敏感性

sensitization
/ˌsensətaɪˈzeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：致敏、敏化（医学）；（对某问题的）唤起意识、提高认识。英式也拼作 sensitisation。
① Repeated contact with the chemical can lead to sensitization and a skin rash.
= 反复接触那种化学品可能导致过敏，出现皮疹。
sensitization = 致敏
② The campaign aims at the sensitization of young people to the dangers of drugs.
= 那场宣传活动旨在让年轻人认识到毒品的危害。
the sensitization of somebody to something = 使某人认识到某事

sensitize
/ˈsensətaɪz/
核心：动词，及物：使认识到、使敏感：sensitize somebody to something；（医学）使致敏。英式也拼作 sensitise。
① The film helped to sensitize the public to the problems of homeless people.
= 那部电影帮助公众认识到无家可归者的困境。
sensitize somebody to something = 使某人认识到某事
sensitize = 使认识到
② Some people become sensitized to certain foods and react badly when they eat them.
= 有些人对某些食物变得过敏，吃了就有不良反应。
become sensitized to something = 对某物变得过敏

sensor
/ˈsensə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：传感器、感应器：探测光、热、动作等的装置。
① The lights come on automatically when the sensor detects someone walking past.
= 感应器一探测到有人走过，灯就自动亮了。
a sensor = 传感器
② Modern cars have sensors that warn the driver when another car is too close.
= 现代汽车装有传感器，别的车靠得太近时会提醒司机。
sensors = 传感器
常构成复合词：a motion sensor、a heat sensor。

sensory
/ˈsensəri/
核心：形容词：感觉的、感官的：sensory organs、sensory experience。
① In today's biology lesson we learned that the eyes, ears and nose are all sensory organs.
= 眼睛、耳朵和鼻子都是感觉器官。
sensory organs = 感觉器官
sensory = 感官的
② The museum has a sensory room where children can touch, hear and smell different things.
= 那座博物馆有一间感官体验室，孩子们可以触摸、聆听、闻各种东西。
a sensory room = 感官体验室
③ Visitors to the market are hit by a sensory overload of colours, noise and smells.
= 走进那个市场，游客们立刻被各种颜色、声音和气味淹没。
a sensory overload = 感官超载

sensual
/ˈsenʃuəl/
核心：形容词：感官享受的、肉体上的；性感的、肉欲的：sensual pleasure。副词 sensually 是性感地。名词 sensuality 另立条。和 sensuous 的分别：sensual 更偏肉体、性的方面。
① The film is about a sensual love affair between a painter and his model.
= 那部电影讲的是一位画家和他的模特之间一段肉欲的恋情。
a sensual love affair = 肉欲的恋情
sensual = 性感的
② She danced sensually to the slow music, and everyone stopped to watch.
= 她随着慢节奏的音乐性感地起舞，大家都停下来看。
sensually = 性感地
③ For him, cooking was a sensual pleasure, full of colours, smells and tastes.
= 对他来说，做饭是一种感官享受，充满色彩、香味和滋味。
a sensual pleasure = 感官享受

sensuality
/ˌsenʃuˈæləti/
核心：名词，不可数：性感、肉感；对感官享受的追求。
① The actress was famous for her sensuality and her deep, slow voice.
= 那位女演员以性感和低沉缓慢的嗓音出名。
sensuality = 性感
② The poems celebrate the sensuality of summer, with its heat, fruit and long evenings.
= 那些诗歌颂夏天的感官之美：炎热、果实和漫长的傍晚。
the sensuality of something = 某物的感官之美

sensuous
/ˈsenʃuəs/
核心：形容词：给人美感的、愉悦感官的：sensuous music、sensuous colours。副词 sensuously 是悦目地、愉悦感官地。名词 sensuousness 另立条。比 sensual 更偏美感，不一定和性有关。
① The painting is full of sensuous colours, deep reds and soft golden yellows.
= 那幅画色彩悦目，有深红色，也有柔和的金黄色。
sensuous colours = 悦目的色彩
sensuous = 愉悦感官的
② The silk dress moved sensuously as she walked slowly across the crowded room towards the piano.
= 她走过房间时，那条丝绸裙子柔美地飘动着。
sensuously = 柔美地

sensuousness
/ˈsenʃuəsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：愉悦感官、美感。
① Critics praised the sensuousness of the writer's descriptions of food and gardens.
= 评论家称赞那位作家对食物和花园的描写充满感官之美。
the sensuousness of something = 某物的感官之美
sensuousness = 美感
② The sensuousness of the music made the audience close their eyes and listen.
= 那音乐美妙动人，听众闭上眼睛聆听。
the sensuousness of the music = 音乐的美妙

sentence
/ˈsentəns/
核心：名词，可数：（语法）句子：write a sentence；（法庭的）判决、判刑：a prison sentence、pass sentence on somebody、serve a sentence、a life sentence、a death sentence。动词，及物：判决、宣判：sentence somebody to something。
① Each sentence in your story should begin with a capital letter and end with a full stop.
= 你故事里的每个句子都要以大写字母开头、以句号结尾。
sentence = 句子
语法里 = 句子
② The judge gave him a sentence of five years for robbing the bank in the city centre.
= 法官因他抢劫市中心那家银行判了他五年刑。
sentence = 判决
法庭上 = 判刑
③ He is serving a life sentence for murder and will probably never leave prison.
= 他因谋杀正在服无期徒刑，很可能永远出不了监狱。
serve a sentence = 服刑
a life sentence = 无期徒刑
④ The court sentenced her to two years in prison for stealing money from her employer.
= 法院因她偷雇主的钱判了她两年监禁。
sentence somebody to something = 判某人…刑
⑤ In our grammar class, the teacher explained that an adverb at the start of a line that comments on the whole idea is called a sentence adverb.
= 语法课上，老师解释说，放在句首、评述整个意思的副词叫作句子副词。
a sentence adverb = 句子副词
⑥ In some countries, the judge can still pass a death sentence on people who kill police officers.
= 在一些国家，法官对杀害警察的人仍可判处死刑。
a death sentence = 死刑
pass sentence on somebody = 对某人宣判

sententious
/senˈtenʃəs/
核心：形容词：好说教的、爱讲大道理的（贬义）。副词 sententiously 是说教地。
① Nobody enjoyed his sententious speech about how young people today have no respect for anything.
= 他那番说现在的年轻人什么都不尊重的说教演讲，没人爱听。
a sententious speech = 说教的讲话
sententious = 好说教的
② "Money cannot buy happiness," my uncle said sententiously, as he sat down in his expensive new car.
= 我叔叔一边坐进他那辆昂贵的新车，一边一本正经地说教道："金钱买不到幸福。"
sententiously = 说教地

sentient
/ˈsentiənt/
核心：形容词：有感觉能力的、有知觉的：sentient beings。
① Many people believe that all sentient beings, including animals, have the right not to suffer.
= 许多人认为，包括动物在内的一切有知觉的生命都有不受痛苦的权利。
sentient beings = 有知觉的生命
sentient = 有知觉的
② In the science fiction film, the computer slowly becomes sentient and starts to make its own decisions.
= 在那部科幻电影里，电脑慢慢有了知觉，开始自己做决定。
become sentient = 产生知觉

sentiment
/ˈsentɪmənt/
核心：名词：（对某事的）看法、意见、情绪（可数或不可数）：public sentiment、anti-war sentiment；（不可数）伤感、多愁善感（常贬义）；（可数）（表达出来的）想法、心意：I agree with the sentiment。
① Public sentiment turned against the government after the price of bread doubled in one month.
= 面包价格一个月内翻了一番之后，公众情绪转而反对政府。
public sentiment = 公众情绪
sentiment = 看法、情绪
② There is no room for sentiment in business, so he sold his grandfather's old shop to the highest bidder.
= 做生意容不得感情用事，所以他把祖父的老店卖给了出价最高的人。
there is no room for sentiment = 容不得感情用事
③ "Thanks for the card. I share the sentiment, even if the poem is terrible," she laughed at her birthday party.
= 她在生日聚会上笑着说："谢谢你的卡片。诗写得糟透了，但心意我领了。"
share the sentiment = 有同感、领会心意

sentimental
/ˌsentɪˈmentl/
核心：形容词：（东西）有情感价值的：sentimental value；（人）多愁善感的、重感情的：get sentimental about something；（电影、书）伤感的、煽情的（贬义）。副词 sentimentally 是伤感地、从感情上。
① The ring is not worth much money, but it has great sentimental value because it was my mother's.
= 那枚戒指不值多少钱，但很有纪念意义，因为它是我母亲的。
sentimental value = 情感价值
sentimental = 有情感价值的
② My father always gets sentimental about his school days when he has had a glass of wine.
= 我父亲喝一杯酒之后总会对上学的日子感慨万千。
get sentimental about something = 对某事感怀伤情
③ The film was so sentimental that half the people in the cinema were crying by the end.
= 那部电影太煽情了，散场时影院里一半的人都在哭。
a sentimental film = 煽情的电影
④ She is sentimentally attached to the old house where she grew up, so she cannot sell it.
= 她从感情上舍不得长大的那座老房子，所以没法卖掉它。
sentimentally attached = 感情上舍不得
sentimentally = 从感情上

sentimentalist
/ˌsentɪˈmentəlɪst/
核心：名词，可数：多愁善感的人、感情用事的人。
① My grandmother is a sentimentalist who keeps every birthday card she has ever received in a box.
= 我奶奶是个重感情的人，收到过的每张生日卡都存在一个盒子里。
a sentimentalist = 多愁善感的人
sentimentalist = 感情用事的人
② Only a sentimentalist would cry at the closing of the old railway station, his brother said with a smile.
= 他哥哥笑着说，只有多愁善感的人才会因为老火车站关门而哭。
only a sentimentalist would = 只有多愁善感的人才会

sentimentality
/ˌsentɪmenˈtæləti/
核心：名词，不可数：多愁善感、伤感、煽情（常贬义）。
① The book tells the sad story of a dying child without any sentimentality, which makes it even more moving.
= 那本书讲一个垂死孩子的悲伤故事，丝毫不煽情，这反而更感人。
without sentimentality = 不煽情
sentimentality = 伤感、煽情
② Critics complained about the sentimentality of the ending, where the lost dog finds its way home in the snow.
= 评论家抱怨结尾太煽情：走失的狗在雪地里找到了回家的路。
the sentimentality of something = 某物的煽情

sentimentalize
/ˌsentɪˈmentəlaɪz/
核心：动词，及物：把…感情化、美化：sentimentalize the past。英式也拼作 sentimentalise。
① Old people often sentimentalize the past and forget how hard life was without washing machines.
= 老人常常美化过去，忘了没有洗衣机的日子有多苦。
sentimentalize the past = 美化过去
sentimentalize = 美化
② The writer refused to sentimentalize the lives of the poor farmers in her novel.
= 那位作家在小说里拒绝把贫苦农民的生活写得温情脉脉。
refuse to sentimentalize something = 拒绝美化某事

sentinel
/ˈsentɪnl/
核心：名词，可数：哨兵、守卫（书面）；（比喻）守护者：stand sentinel。
① Two soldiers stood as sentinels at the gate of the castle all through the cold night.
= 两名士兵整个寒夜都在城堡门口站岗放哨。
a sentinel = 哨兵
sentinel = 守卫
② A tall old oak tree stands sentinel at the entrance to the village, as it has done for three hundred years.
= 一棵高大的老橡树守在村口，三百年来一直如此。
stand sentinel = 守卫、矗立守护

sentry
/ˈsentri/
核心：名词，可数：哨兵、岗哨：on sentry duty；（复数 sentries）。
① The sentry at the gate asked to see our passes before he would let us into the army camp.
= 门口的哨兵要看了我们的通行证才肯放我们进军营。
a sentry = 哨兵
② Two young soldiers were on sentry duty outside the general's tent all night.
= 两个年轻士兵整夜在将军的帐篷外站岗。
on sentry duty = 站岗
③ The guard stepped out of his sentry box, a small wooden hut beside the palace gate, to stop the car.
= 警卫从宫门旁那间小木屋似的岗亭里走出来，拦下了那辆车。
a sentry box = 岗亭

sepal
/ˈsepl/
核心：名词，可数：（植物）萼片：花瓣外面保护花蕾的绿色小叶。
① Before the flower opens, the green sepals protect the soft petals inside from the cold.
= 花开之前，绿色的萼片保护着里面柔嫩的花瓣不受寒冷。
sepals = 萼片
② In the biology lesson, we counted the sepals and petals of a buttercup under a magnifying glass.
= 生物课上，我们在放大镜下数了毛茛的萼片和花瓣。
sepal = 萼片

separable
/ˈsepərəbl/
核心：形容词：可分开的、可分离的：be separable from something；（语法）可分的：a separable verb。名词 separability 是可分离性。
① In this case, the question of money is not separable from the question of who owns the land.
= 在这件事上，钱的问题和土地归谁所有的问题分不开。
be separable from something = 可以和某物分开
separable = 可分开的
② In German grammar, a separable verb splits into two parts when you use it in a sentence.
= 在德语语法里，可分动词用在句子里时会拆成两部分。
a separable verb = 可分动词
③ Scientists are still arguing about the separability of the mind and the body.
= 科学家们仍在争论心灵和身体能否分离。
separability = 可分离性

separate
/ˈseprət/（形）/ˈsepəreɪt/（动）
核心：形容词：分开的、单独的：keep A separate from B、a separate room；不同的、各别的：on two separate occasions。动词：（使）分开、分离：separate A from B；（夫妻）分居：they have separated；区分、区别：separate A from B。副词 separately 是分开地、单独地。
① We keep the raw meat separate from the vegetables in the fridge so that it does not make anyone ill.
= 我们在冰箱里把生肉和蔬菜分开放，免得有人吃坏肚子。
keep A separate from B = 把 A 和 B 分开
separate = 分开的
② The children sleep in separate rooms now, because they kept fighting at bedtime.
= 孩子们现在分房睡，因为他们老是在睡觉时打架。
separate rooms = 各自的房间
③ The police officer had stopped him for driving too fast on two separate occasions that month.
= 那个月他已经有两次分别因为开车太快被那名警察拦下。
on two separate occasions = 在两个不同的场合
④ Separate the eggs and put the yellow parts into a bowl with the sugar.
= 把蛋清和蛋黄分开，蛋黄放进碗里和糖一起。
separate = 使分开
separate A from B = 把 A 和 B 分开
⑤ Her parents separated when she was six, and she lived with her mother after that.
= 她六岁时父母分居了，后来她跟母亲生活。
separate = 分居
⑥ It is important to separate facts from rumours when you read the news online.
= 在网上看新闻时，区分事实和谣言很重要。
separate facts from rumours = 区分事实和谣言
⑦ The two brothers arrived separately, one by train and one by car.
= 兄弟俩是分头到的，一个坐火车，一个开车。
separately = 分开地
⑧ For many years in the American South, the law kept black and white children in schools that were separate but equal, though they never really were equal.
= 在美国南方，法律多年来让黑人和白人孩子分校上学，号称隔离但平等，其实从未真正平等。
separate but equal = 隔离但平等
⑨ In parts of Canada, Catholic families can send their children to a separate school paid for by the government.
= 在加拿大部分地区，天主教家庭可以送孩子上由政府出资的独立学校。
a separate school = （加拿大的）天主教公立学校
⑩ At the recycling centre, workers separate out the glass, paper and plastic before anything goes to the factory.
= 在回收站，工人先把玻璃、纸和塑料分拣出来，然后才送去工厂。
separate out = 分拣出来、分离出来
separate out 多指从混合物里分离出某一部分。

separated
/ˈsepəreɪtɪd/
核心：形容词：（夫妻）分居的：be separated from your husband；（和家人）失散的：be separated from somebody。
① She has been separated from her husband for two years, but they are not divorced yet.
= 她和丈夫分居两年了，但还没离婚。
be separated from your husband = 和丈夫分居
separated = 分居的
② The little boy got separated from his parents in the crowd at the football match.
= 那个小男孩在足球赛的人群中和父母走散了。
get separated from somebody = 和某人走散

separateness
/ˈseprətnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：分离、独立、隔离感。
① Living alone in a foreign city, she felt a sense of separateness from everyone around her.
= 独自住在一座外国城市里，她觉得自己和周围所有人都隔着一层。
a sense of separateness = 隔离感
separateness = 分离、隔离
② The island has kept its separateness from the mainland, with its own language and customs.
= 那座岛一直保持着和大陆的不同，有自己的语言和风俗。
keep its separateness = 保持独立性

separates
/ˈseprəts/
核心：名词，复数：（可单独搭配的）单件服装：如上衣、裙子、裤子。
① The shop sells separates, so you can buy a jacket in one size and trousers in another.
= 那家店卖单件服装，所以你可以买一个尺码的夹克和另一个尺码的裤子。
separates = 单件服装
② For the holiday she packed a few simple separates that she could wear together in different ways.
= 她度假时装了几件简单的单品，可以换着搭配穿。
simple separates = 简单的单品

separation
/ˌsepəˈreɪʃn/
核心：名词：分开、分离（不可数或可数）：the separation of A from B；离别、分别（可数或不可数）；（夫妻）分居（可数）：a legal separation。
① The constitution says there must be a separation between the church and the state.
= 宪法规定教会和国家必须分开。
separation = 分开
the separation of A from B = A 和 B 的分离
② After a separation of twenty years, the two sisters met again at the airport and cried.
= 分别二十年之后，两姐妹在机场重逢，哭了起来。
a separation of twenty years = 分别二十年
③ They decided on a trial separation to see whether they really wanted a divorce.
= 他们决定先试着分居，看看是不是真的想离婚。
a trial separation = 试分居
④ Young children often suffer from separation anxiety when they start nursery and their parents leave them.
= 小孩刚上托儿所、父母一走开时，常会出现分离焦虑。
separation anxiety = 分离焦虑

separatism
/ˈseprətɪzəm/
核心：名词，不可数：分离主义、分裂主义：主张一个地区或群体脱离国家独立。
① The government is worried that separatism is growing in the northern region, where many people speak a different language.
= 政府担心北部地区的分离主义在增长，那里很多人说另一种语言。
separatism = 分离主义
② Years of separatism and violence have left the region poor and divided.
= 多年的分裂主义和暴力让那个地区贫穷而分裂。
years of separatism = 多年的分裂主义

separatist
/ˈseprətɪst/
核心：名词，可数：分离主义者、分裂主义者。形容词：分离主义的：a separatist movement。
① The separatists want the region to become an independent country with its own flag.
= 分离主义者希望那个地区成为有自己旗帜的独立国家。
a separatist = 分离主义者
separatist = 分离主义的
② A separatist movement in the mountains has been fighting the army for more than ten years.
= 山区的一个分离主义运动已经和军队打了十多年。
a separatist movement = 分离主义运动

separator
/ˈsepəreɪtə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：分离器、分离机：如把奶油从牛奶中分出来的机器。
① On the old farm, a cream separator was used every morning to take the cream off the fresh milk.
= 在那个老农场上，每天早晨都用奶油分离机把奶油从鲜奶里分出来。
a cream separator = 奶油分离器
separator = 分离器
② The engine has an oil separator that stops oil from getting into the air outside.
= 那台发动机装有油分离器，防止油漏进外面的空气里。
an oil separator = 油分离器

Sephardi
/sɪˈfɑːdi/
核心：名词，可数：塞法迪犹太人：祖先来自西班牙、葡萄牙的犹太人（复数 Sephardim）。形容词 Sephardic 是塞法迪犹太人的。比较 Ashkenazi（德系犹太人）。
① Her grandfather was a Sephardi whose family had left Spain five hundred years ago and settled in Turkey.
= 她祖父是塞法迪犹太人，祖辈五百年前离开西班牙，定居在土耳其。
a Sephardi = 塞法迪犹太人
② The old Sephardic songs are still sung in a language that mixes Spanish and Hebrew.
= 那些古老的塞法迪犹太歌曲至今仍用一种混合了西班牙语和希伯来语的语言来唱。
Sephardic = 塞法迪犹太人的

sepia
/ˈsiːpiə/
核心：名词，不可数：深褐色、乌贼墨色（旧照片的颜色）。形容词：深褐色的：a sepia photograph。
① On the wall hung an old sepia photograph of my great-grandparents on their wedding day.
= 墙上挂着一张我曾祖父母结婚那天的深褐色老照片。
a sepia photograph = 深褐色老照片
sepia = 深褐色的
② You can use this app to turn your holiday pictures into sepia, so they look a hundred years old.
= 你可以用这个应用把度假照片变成深褐色，看起来像一百年前的。
turn something into sepia = 把某物变成深褐色

sepsis
/ˈsepsɪs/
核心：名词，不可数：脓毒症、败血症：细菌感染扩散到血液、危及生命的病症。
① The small cut on his foot became infected, and within two days he was in hospital with sepsis.
= 他脚上的小伤口感染了，不到两天就因脓毒症住进了医院。
sepsis = 脓毒症
② Doctors say that early treatment with strong medicine is the best way to stop sepsis from killing a patient.
= 医生说，及早用强效药物治疗是防止脓毒症致命的最好办法。
stop sepsis = 阻止脓毒症

September
/sepˈtembə(r)/
核心：名词，可数或不可数：九月：in September、on 5 September、last / next September。月份名首字母大写。
① The new school year starts in September, so we always buy new shoes at the end of August.
= 新学年九月开始，所以我们总在八月底买新鞋。
September = 九月
in September = 在九月
② My sister is getting married on 12 September in the little church near our grandmother's farm.
= 我姐姐九月十二日要在我们奶奶农场附近的小教堂结婚。
on 12 September = 在九月十二日
③ Last September we had a week of hot sunny weather, and the beaches were full again.
= 去年九月有一个星期天气又热又晴，海滩上又挤满了人。
last September = 去年九月

septet
/sepˈtet/
核心：名词，可数：七重奏、七重唱（乐曲）；七人乐团、七重奏乐队。
① The jazz septet played every Friday night in a small bar near the harbour.
= 那个七人爵士乐团每周五晚上在港口附近的一家小酒吧演出。
a jazz septet = 七人爵士乐团
septet = 七人乐团
② Beethoven wrote a septet for violin, viola, cello, double bass, clarinet, horn and bassoon.
= 贝多芬写过一首七重奏，用小提琴、中提琴、大提琴、低音提琴、单簧管、圆号和大管演奏。
a septet = 七重奏

septic
/ˈseptɪk/
核心：形容词：（伤口）感染的、化脓的：go septic；（医学）脓毒性的。
① He did not clean the cut on his hand, and after a few days it went septic and was very painful.
= 他没清理手上的伤口，几天后伤口化脓了，疼得厉害。
go septic = 化脓
septic = 感染的
② The nurse warned that a septic wound can make you very ill if you do not get it treated quickly.
= 护士提醒说，伤口感染如不及时治疗，会让人病得很重。
a septic wound = 感染的伤口
③ Houses in the countryside that are not connected to the town drains usually have a septic tank in the garden.
= 乡下没接上镇里下水道的房子，院子里通常有个化粪池。
a septic tank = 化粪池

septicaemia
/ˌseptɪˈsiːmiə/
核心：名词，不可数：败血症（医学）。美式拼作 septicemia。
① The old farmer died of septicaemia after a dirty piece of wire cut his leg in the field.
= 那位老农在地里被一截脏铁丝划伤了腿，后来死于败血症。
septicaemia = 败血症
② Before modern medicine, septicaemia was one of the most common causes of death after an operation.
= 在现代医学出现之前，败血症是手术后最常见的死因之一。
die of septicaemia = 死于败血症

septuagenarian
/ˌseptʃuədʒəˈneəriən/
核心：名词，可数：七旬老人（70 至 79 岁的人）。形容词：七十多岁的。
① The marathon was won by a septuagenarian who had only started running when he retired from his job.
= 那场马拉松的冠军是位七十多岁的老人，他退休后才开始跑步。
a septuagenarian = 七旬老人
② Her septuagenarian aunt still swims in the cold lake every morning before breakfast.
= 她那位七十多岁的姑姑每天早饭前仍然去冰冷的湖里游泳。
septuagenarian = 七十多岁的

septum
/ˈseptəm/
核心：名词，可数：（解剖）隔膜、中隔：如鼻中隔（复数 septa）。
① The boxer broke his nose and damaged his septum, so he had trouble breathing at night.
= 那名拳击手鼻子骨折，鼻中隔受损，晚上呼吸困难。
septum = 中隔
② In the biology lesson we learned that the septum of the heart, a thin wall of muscle, keeps the left side apart from the right side.
= 生物课上我们学到，心中隔是一层薄薄的肌肉壁，把心脏的左右两边隔开。
the septum of the heart = 心中隔

septuplet
/sepˈtjuːplət/
