slew
/sluː/
核心：名词，单数（口语）：大量、许多：a slew of something。动词：（使）突然转向、打滑：slew round。也是 slay 的过去式。
① The company has brought out a whole slew of new products this year.
= 那家公司今年推出了一大批新产品。
a slew of something = 大量某物
slew = 大量
② The little boat slewed round in the strong wind and nearly hit the harbour wall.
= 那条小船在大风中猛地打了个转，差点撞上港口的堤墙。
slew round = 猛地打转

slice
/slaɪs/
核心：名词，可数：（切下的）薄片、片：a slice of bread；（某物的）一部分、份额：a slice of the profits；（网球、高尔夫等的）削球、斜切球；（厨房用的）锅铲。动词：把…切成片：slice the bread；切、割：slice through something；（球类）打削球。习语：a slice of life 是生活的真实写照。
① Cut the cake into eight slices so that everyone can have one.
= 把蛋糕切成八块，好让每人都有一块。
slice = 片
② At breakfast, the waiter asked if I would like a slice of toast with my coffee.
= 早餐时，服务员问我要不要来一片吐司配咖啡。
a slice of = 一片
后接面包、蛋糕、肉、柠檬等能切片的东西。
③ He made a sandwich with two slices of bread, some cheese and a tomato.
= 他用两片面包、一些奶酪和一个番茄做了个三明治。
a slice of bread = 一片面包
近义对照：
a piece of cake = 一块蛋糕
④ After a very good year, the workers want a bigger slice of the company's profits.
= 经过一个很好的年头，工人们想分得公司利润中更大的一份。
a slice of the profits = 利润的一份
⑤ For the soup, slice the onions thinly and fry them slowly in a little butter.
= 做这道汤要把洋葱切成薄片，用一点黄油慢慢煎。
slice something thinly = 把某物切成薄片
⑥ The knife was so sharp that it sliced through the rope at once.
= 那把刀太锋利了，一下子就把绳子割断了。
slice through something = 割断某物
⑦ In the spring, the farmer sliced away the dead branches with a long knife.
= 春天，农场主用一把长刀把枯枝砍掉了。
slice away = 切掉、砍掉
⑧ She sliced off the end of the loaf and gave it to the ducks.
= 她切下面包的一头喂鸭子。
slice off = 切下
⑨ The film is a slice of life in a small town in the 1950s.
= 那部电影真实地描绘了二十世纪五十年代一个小镇的生活。
a slice of life = 生活的真实写照

slick
/slɪk/
核心：形容词：（做得）熟练流畅的、精彩的：a slick performance；（贬义）圆滑的、花言巧语的：a slick salesman；（表面）光滑的、滑溜的。名词，可数：（水面上的）浮油：an oil slick。副词 slickly 是流畅地、圆滑地。
① The band gave a slick performance, and the audience loved every song.
= 乐队的演出流畅精彩，观众喜欢每一首歌。
a slick performance = 流畅精彩的演出
slick = 熟练流畅的
② Don't trust that slick salesman; he will say anything to sell you a car.
= 别相信那个油嘴滑舌的推销员，为了卖车给你他什么话都说得出来。
a slick salesman = 油嘴滑舌的推销员
③ On the way home, the roads were slick with ice, so we drove very slowly.
= 回家路上，路面结了冰很滑，所以我们开得很慢。
slick with ice = 结冰很滑
④ The oil slick from the ship spread along the coast and killed hundreds of birds.
= 那艘船漏出的浮油沿海岸蔓延，害死了几百只鸟。
an oil slick = 浮油
⑤ The advert was slickly made, but it didn't tell you anything about the product.
= 那则广告拍得很精致，却没告诉你产品的任何信息。
slickly = 流畅地、精致地

slicker
/ˈslɪkə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（美式）：（长的）雨衣；（口语）城里来的精明人（city slicker）。
① Out on the boat, the fishermen wore yellow slickers to keep dry in the storm.
= 在船上，渔民们穿着黄色雨衣，在暴风雨中不被淋湿。
yellow slickers = 黄色雨衣
slicker = 雨衣
② The farmers laughed at the city slicker who did not know how to milk a cow.
= 农民们嘲笑那个不会挤牛奶的城里人。
a city slicker = 城里来的精明人

slickness
/ˈslɪknəs/
核心：名词，不可数：熟练流畅；（贬义）圆滑；光滑。
① The slickness of the show made it hard to believe that the actors were only students.
= 那场演出非常流畅，很难相信演员们只是学生。
the slickness of the show = 演出的流畅
slickness = 熟练流畅
② Many older voters did not trust the slickness of the young politician on television.
= 许多年长选民不信任那个年轻政客在电视上的圆滑。
the slickness of somebody = 某人的圆滑

slide
/slaɪd/
核心：动词（slid, slid）：滑动、滑行：slide on the ice；（使）悄悄地移动：slide something into something；（价格、水平）逐渐下降、下滑；（不知不觉地）陷入：slide into debt。名词，可数：滑梯；（幻灯片）：a slide show；下滑、下降；（显微镜的）载玻片；（英式）发夹。习语：let something slide 是放任不管、不追究。
① On the first frosty morning, the children slid on the ice in the playground.
= 第一个下霜的早晨，孩子们在操场的冰上滑来滑去。
slide = 滑
② In the history lesson, the teacher showed us slides of her trip to Egypt.
= 历史课上，老师给我们看她去埃及旅行的幻灯片。
slide = 幻灯片
③ After dinner, my grandfather gave a slide show of his holiday photos.
= 晚饭后，我祖父放了一场度假照片的幻灯片。
slide show = 幻灯片放映
④ He was late again, but I decided to let it slide this time.
= 他又迟到了，不过这次我决定不追究了。
let it slide = 别追究了
口语，多用于对小错误网开一面。
⑤ Late at night, she slid the letter under his door and walked quietly away.
= 深夜，她把信从他门底下塞进去，然后悄悄走开了。
slide something under something = 把某物塞到某物下面
⑥ The price of oil has slid to its lowest level for ten years.
= 石油价格已经跌到十年来的最低水平。
slide to something = 下滑至某水平
⑦ The little children took turns going down the slide in the park.
= 孩子们轮流从公园的滑梯上滑下来。
the slide = 滑梯
⑧ Before there were calculators, engineers used a slide rule to do difficult sums.
= 在有计算器之前，工程师们用计算尺做复杂的运算。
a slide rule = 计算尺

slider
/ˈslaɪdə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：（电脑屏幕、机器上的）滑块、滑动控制钮；（美式）小汉堡；（棒球）滑球。
① On the screen, move the slider to the right to make the sound louder.
= 在屏幕上把滑块向右拖，声音就会变大。
the slider = 滑块
slider = 滑动控制钮
② We ordered a plate of mini sliders and chips at the bar.
= 我们在酒吧点了一盘迷你小汉堡和薯条。
mini sliders = 迷你小汉堡

slight
/slaɪt/
核心：形容词：轻微的、微小的、少量的：a slight cold、a slight change；（人）瘦小的、纤细的。名词，可数：怠慢、轻视：a slight on somebody。动词，及物：怠慢、轻视。习语：not in the slightest 是一点也不。副词 slightly 是略微、稍微。
① I have a slight cold, but I'm well enough to go to work.
= 我有点小感冒，不过还能去上班。
slight = 轻微的
② The new shoes are slightly too big, so I'll take them back to the shop.
= 那双新鞋稍微大了点，所以我要拿回店里去换。
slightly = 略微
slightly 修饰形容词、比较级：slightly better、slightly too big。
③ Because of the rain, there has been a slight change in the plan for the school trip.
= 因为下雨，学校出游的计划有一点小小的变化。
a slight change = 小小的变化
④ When I asked if she was angry about the broken window, she said she was not worried in the slightest.
= 我问她是不是为打碎的窗户生气，她说她一点也不在意。
not in the slightest = 一点也不
⑤ She was a slight girl, but she could run faster than anyone in her class.
= 她是个瘦小的女孩，可她跑得比班上谁都快。
a slight girl = 瘦小的女孩
⑥ He took it as a personal slight when he was not invited to the wedding.
= 他没被邀请参加婚礼，觉得是对自己的怠慢。
a personal slight = 对个人的怠慢

slighting
/ˈslaɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：轻视的、怠慢的、不尊重的：slighting remarks。
① He made some slighting remarks about her cooking in front of the guests.
= 他当着客人的面说了些贬低她厨艺的话。
slighting remarks = 轻视的话
slighting = 轻视的
② The newspaper's slighting review of her first novel hurt the young writer deeply.
= 那份报纸对她第一部小说的轻蔑书评深深伤害了那位年轻作家。
a slighting review = 轻蔑的书评

slim
/slɪm/
核心：形容词：苗条的、纤细的：a slim figure；（可能性、数量）微小的、不大的：a slim chance；（东西）薄的、细的：a slim book。动词（slimmed, slimmed）（英式）：减肥、变苗条；缩减：slim down。
① She is tall and slim and looks good in anything she wears.
= 她又高又苗条，穿什么都好看。
slim = 苗条的
② There is only a slim chance that the plane will leave on time in this fog.
= 这么大的雾，飞机准时起飞的可能性很小。
slim = 微小的
这一支多和 chance、hope、majority 连用。
a slim chance = 微小的可能
③ In the months before his wedding, he slimmed down and lost ten kilos.
= 婚礼前的几个月里，他减肥瘦了十公斤。
slim down = 减肥、缩减
④ The company plans to slim down its staff from five hundred to three hundred.
= 那家公司计划把员工从五百人裁减到三百人。
slim down the staff = 裁减员工
⑤ She wrote a slim book of poems about her childhood by the sea.
= 她写了一本薄薄的诗集，讲她在海边的童年。
a slim book = 薄薄的书

slime
/slaɪm/
核心：名词，不可数：黏液、黏糊糊的东西：the slime of a snail。
① The snail left a trail of shiny slime across the garden path.
= 蜗牛在花园小路上留下了一道闪亮的黏液痕迹。
slime = 黏液

slip
/slɪp/
核心：动词（slipped, slipped）：滑倒、滑跤：slip on the ice；滑落、脱落；悄悄地溜走、溜进：slip out；悄悄地放、塞：slip something into something；（水平）下降、下滑；（迅速地）穿上、脱下：slip on a coat。名词，可数：滑倒；小错、疏忽：a slip of the tongue；纸条、单据：a slip of paper；（女式）衬裙。习语：let slip 是无意中说出；give somebody the slip 是甩掉某人；slip your mind 是忘记。
① The cleaner told us to be careful in the hall, because the wet floor might make us slip.
= 清洁工叫我们在走廊里小心，因为地板湿了，可能会让人滑倒。
slip = 滑倒
② I meant to call you yesterday about the party, but it completely slipped my mind.
= 我昨天本想打电话跟你说派对的事，可完全忘了。
slip one's mind = 忘记
③ Write your name on a slip of paper and put it in the box.
= 把你的名字写在一张纸条上，放进盒子里。
slip = 纸条
④ Feeling tired, she slipped away from the party early without saying goodbye.
= 她觉得累了，没打招呼就早早从派对上溜走了。
slip away = 溜走
⑤ In the classroom, he slipped the note into my pocket when nobody was looking.
= 在教室里，他趁没人注意，把纸条塞进了我的口袋。
slip something into something = 把某物悄悄塞进某处
⑥ I called her by her sister's name; it was just a slip of the tongue.
= 我叫了她姐姐的名字，只是一时口误。
a slip of the tongue = 口误
⑦ At dinner, she let slip that she was moving to Canada with her new boyfriend.
= 晚饭时，她无意中说出自己要和新男朋友搬去加拿大。
let slip = 无意中说出
⑧ The thief gave the police the slip by running into the crowded market.
= 小偷钻进拥挤的市场，甩掉了警察。
give somebody the slip = 甩掉某人
⑨ Tie a slip knot so that you can untie the rope quickly.
= 打一个活结，这样你能很快解开绳子。
a slip knot = 活结
⑩ We took the slip road off the motorway to find a petrol station.
= 我们走匝道下了高速公路去找加油站。
a slip road = （英式）匝道
⑪ To the office he wore a pair of brown slip-on shoes with no laces.
= 他穿着一双不用系带的棕色套脚鞋去上班。
slip-on = 套穿的、无带的
⑫ I'm just going to slip out for a coffee; I'll be back in ten minutes.
= 我出去买杯咖啡，十分钟就回来。
slip out = 悄悄出去；（话）脱口而出
⑬ The bank slipped up and sent my letters to the wrong address.
= 银行出了差错，把我的信寄错了地址。
slip up = 出差错

slog
/slɒɡ/
核心：动词（slogged, slogged）（口语）：苦干、埋头苦干：slog away at something；艰难地走、跋涉：slog through the mud；（板球、拳击中）猛击。名词，单数或不可数：苦差事、艰难的跋涉。
① She slogged away at her essay all weekend and finished it on Sunday night.
= 她整个周末都在埋头写论文，星期天晚上终于写完了。
slog away at something = 埋头苦干某事
slog = 苦干
② We slogged through the mud for three hours before we reached the farm.
= 我们在泥地里跋涉了三个小时才到达农场。
slog through something = 在某处艰难跋涉
③ Learning all the vocabulary for the French exam was a hard slog.
= 背下法语考试要用的所有词汇是件苦差事。
a hard slog = 苦差事

slogan
/ˈsləʊɡən/
核心：名词，可数：口号、标语；广告语。
① On Saturday, the protesters marched through the city shouting slogans against the war.
= 星期六，抗议者们游行穿过城市，高喊反战口号。
slogans = 口号
slogan = 口号
② The company's new advertising slogan is short, funny and easy to remember.
= 那家公司的新广告语简短、有趣又好记。
slogan = 口号、广告语
近义对照：
motto = 座右铭
catchphrase = 招牌台词

sloganeering
/ˌsləʊɡəˈnɪərɪŋ/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：喊口号、标语式宣传（只有口号没有实质内容）。
① Voters are tired of political sloganeering and want real answers to their problems.
= 选民们厌倦了政治口号，想要真正解决问题的答案。
political sloganeering = 政治口号
sloganeering = 喊口号
② The speech was full of sloganeering but said nothing about how to pay for the plan.
= 那篇讲话满是口号，却只字未提计划的钱从哪里来。
full of sloganeering = 满是口号

sloop
/sluːp/
核心：名词，可数：单桅帆船。
① An old wooden sloop sailed slowly into the harbour as the sun went down.
= 太阳下山时，一艘旧木单桅帆船缓缓驶进港口。
a sloop = 单桅帆船
sloop = 单桅帆船
② In the eighteenth century, pirates used fast sloops to attack merchant ships.
= 十八世纪，海盗们用快速单桅帆船袭击商船。
fast sloops = 快速单桅帆船

slop
/slɒp/
核心：动词（slopped, slopped）：（液体）溢出、晃出：slop over the edge；使溢出。名词：（复数 slops）泔水、残羹剩饭；（不可数）稀糊糊的食物。slop about / around 是（英式）懒散地闲逛；slop out 是（犯人）倒便桶。
① He walked too fast and the tea slopped over the edge of the cup.
= 他走得太快，茶从杯子边上晃了出来。
slop over something = 从某物溢出
slop = 溢出
② Every evening the farmer fed the kitchen slops to his pigs in the yard.
= 每天傍晚，农场主都把厨房的泔水拿到院子里喂猪。
slops = 泔水
③ After the rain, the children slopped about in the puddles in their wellington boots.
= 雨后，孩子们穿着雨靴在水坑里啪嗒啪嗒地玩。
slop about = 在水里啪嗒啪嗒地走
④ The soup slopped around in the pan as he carried it carefully across the kitchen.
= 他小心翼翼地端着锅穿过厨房，锅里的汤晃来晃去。
slop around = （液体）晃来晃去；（英式）懒散地闲逛
⑤ In the old prisons, the men had to slop out every morning.
= 在旧时的监狱里，犯人每天早上都得倒便桶。
slop out = （犯人）倒便桶

slope
/sləʊp/
核心：名词，可数：斜坡、坡地：a steep slope；（单数）坡度、倾斜；（复数 slopes）滑雪坡：the ski slopes。动词，不及物：倾斜、成斜坡：slope down to something。slope off 是（口语）溜走。
① The house is at the bottom of a steep slope, so it is hard to cycle home.
= 那座房子在一个陡坡底下，所以骑车回家很费劲。
slope = 斜坡
a steep slope = 陡坡
② Behind the old house, the long garden slopes down to the river.
= 那座老房子后面，长长的花园向下倾斜，一直延伸到河边。
slope down to something = 向下倾斜至某处
③ We spent the whole week on the ski slopes in the Alps.
= 我们整整一周都在阿尔卑斯山的滑雪坡上。
the ski slopes = 滑雪坡
④ He sloped off before the end of the meeting so that nobody would ask him to help.
= 他在会议结束前溜走了，免得有人请他帮忙。
slope off = 溜走

sloppiness
/ˈslɒpinəs/
核心：名词，不可数：马虎、草率；邋遢。
① The teacher was annoyed by the sloppiness of his maths homework.
= 老师对他数学作业的马虎很恼火。
the sloppiness of something = 某事的马虎
sloppiness = 马虎
② The report blamed the accident on sloppiness in the safety checks at the factory.
= 那份报告把事故归咎于工厂安全检查上的草率。
sloppiness in something = 某方面的草率

sloppy
/ˈslɒpi/
核心：形容词：（工作）马虎的、草率的：sloppy work；（衣服）宽松的、不整洁的：a sloppy jumper；（食物）太稀的、水分太多的；（感情）过于伤感的。副词 sloppily 是马虎地、邋遢地。
① His work is sloppy and full of mistakes, so the manager wants to talk to him.
= 他的工作马虎，错误百出，所以经理要找他谈话。
sloppy work = 马虎的工作
sloppy = 马虎的
② She likes wearing big sloppy jumpers at home on cold winter evenings.
= 冬天寒冷的晚上，她在家喜欢穿宽松的大毛衣。
a sloppy jumper = 宽松的毛衣
③ The sauce was too sloppy, so I added some flour to make it thicker.
= 酱汁太稀了，所以我加了些面粉让它稠一点。
sloppy = 太稀的
④ The teacher said the essay was sloppily written and had no clear argument.
= 老师说那篇论文写得很草率，没有清楚的论点。
sloppily = 马虎地
⑤ For dinner, the children had sloppy joes, soft rolls filled with minced beef in tomato sauce.
= 晚饭孩子们吃的是肉酱汉堡：软面包夹着番茄酱牛肉末。
a sloppy joe = （美式）肉酱汉堡

slosh
/slɒʃ/
核心：动词：（液体）晃动、溅泼：slosh around；（在水里）啪嗒啪嗒地走：slosh through the water；（随意地）泼、倒：slosh something into something。
① The water sloshed around in the bottom of the boat as we rowed.
= 我们划船时，船底的水晃来晃去。
slosh around = 晃来晃去
slosh = 晃动
② The fishermen sloshed about on the deck in their heavy boots as the waves came over the side.
= 浪头打上船舷，渔民们穿着沉重的靴子在甲板的积水里蹚来蹚去。
slosh about on the deck = 在甲板上蹚水
③ In a hurry, he sloshed some milk into his coffee and drank it quickly.
= 他匆匆忙忙往咖啡里随手倒了些牛奶，很快喝了下去。
slosh something into something = 把某物随手倒进某物

sloshed
/slɒʃt/
核心：形容词（口语）：喝醉的。
① He got sloshed at his brother's wedding and started singing on the table.
= 他在他哥哥的婚礼上喝醉了，站到桌子上唱起歌来。
get sloshed = 喝醉
sloshed = 喝醉的
② By the end of the evening, half the guests were completely sloshed.
= 到晚上结束时，一半客人都喝得烂醉。
completely sloshed = 烂醉

slot
/slɒt/
核心：名词，可数：（投币、插卡等的）狭长孔、缝：put a coin in the slot；（时间表中的）时段、位置：a time slot。动词（slotted, slotted）：（把某物）放入、插入：slot something into something；（被）安排进：slot in。
① Put a coin in the slot and the machine will give you a ticket.
= 往投币口里放一枚硬币，机器就会出一张票。
slot = 细长的口
② The programme has moved to a later time slot on Saturday evenings.
= 那个节目挪到了星期六晚上一个更晚的时段。
slot = 时间档
time slot = 时段
③ He spent all his money on the slot machines at the seaside.
= 他在海边把钱都花在了老虎机上。
slot machines = 老虎机
④ The doctor is very busy, but she can slot you in at four o'clock.
= 医生很忙，不过她可以把你安排在四点。
slot in = 安排进、插进
⑤ The new part slots into the back of the old machine very easily.
= 那个新零件很容易就插进了旧机器的背面。
slot into something = 插进某物

sloth
/sləʊθ/
核心：名词：树懒（可数，一种行动极慢、倒挂在树上的南美动物）；（不可数，书面）懒惰。
① The sloth hung upside down from a branch and hardly moved all day.
= 那只树懒倒挂在一根树枝上，一整天几乎没动。
the sloth = 树懒
sloth = 树懒
② The priest warned that sloth was one of the seven deadly sins.
= 神父告诫说，懒惰是七宗罪之一。
sloth = 懒惰

slothful
/ˈsləʊθfl/
核心：形容词（书面）：懒惰的、懒散的。
① The new manager wanted to get rid of the slothful workers who arrived late every day.
= 新经理想辞退那些天天迟到的懒散员工。
slothful workers = 懒散的员工
slothful = 懒惰的
② After a slothful summer of sleeping late, he found it hard to get up for school.
= 一个夏天都睡懒觉、过得懒懒散散，他发现上学时很难早起。
a slothful summer = 懒散的夏天

slotted
/ˈslɒtɪd/
核心：形容词：有狭缝的、开槽的：a slotted spoon。
① Use a slotted spoon to take the eggs out of the hot water.
= 用漏勺把鸡蛋从热水里捞出来。
a slotted spoon = 漏勺
slotted = 有狭缝的
② The screw has a slotted head, so you need a flat screwdriver.
= 那颗螺丝是一字槽头，所以你需要一把一字螺丝刀。
a slotted head = 一字槽头
